Feedback Report

Your paragraph raises an interesting critical idea: American media promotes a model of success that hides inequality and encourages people to blame individuals rather than society. This is a strong argument, but right now the writing needs clearer structure, more precise language, and better sentence control to make your ideas persuasive.

Main strengths
Strong critical perspective: You question the idea that “everyone has an equal opportunity to succeed.” This gives your essay a clear argumentative direction.
Relevant topic: Media, technology, success, and inequality are connected in a meaningful way.
Ambition: You try to discuss both historical values and modern society, which shows intellectual engagement.

Main issues to revise

A. Clarify your thesis
At the moment, your central claim is present, but it is hard to follow because the first sentence is very long and contains several ideas at once. Your reader needs one clear thesis statement.

For example, your argument seems to be:
American media and modern technology reinforce a false belief that success is available equally to everyone, while ignoring structural inequality and encouraging people to blame the unsuccessful.

Try to state this directly in one sentence near the beginning.

B. Improve organization
Your paragraph moves through several big ideas:
ideology of success in the U.S.
influence of television/media
change in American values
technology and faster life
loss of human connection
blaming the unsuccessful

These ideas are related, but they need a clearer order. A possible structure:

Introduce the dominant ideology: success depends on following social rules.
Show how media spreads this ideology.
Explain why this ideology is misleading: opportunities are not equal.
Discuss how technology and fast-paced life make inequality less visible.
Conclude with the social consequence: people lose empathy and blame individuals.

C. Make sentences shorter and more grammatical
Many sentences are run-ons or comma splices. This makes the essay difficult to read. For example:

> “In the 20th cen, the values of/that the United States was founded on have changed in meaning and sense when applied to modern day America, nothing was ever equal.”

This should be divided into at least two sentences. In academic writing, shorter and clearer sentences are usually stronger than very long ones.

D. Develop ideas with examples
You mention TV commercials and TV programs, but you do not give specific examples. To strengthen your argument, include one or two concrete illustrations:
What kinds of advertisements promote the success ideology?
What kind of TV characters represent the “ideal” American life?
How does media ignore poverty, racial inequality, or class differences?

Examples will make your claims more convincing.

Language and style corrections

Word choice / phrasing
Some expressions are unclear or too informal. Consider these revisions:
“for one to be successful, one must” → “to be successful, a person must”
“popular media everyday” → “popular media every day”
“project models of how one should be” → “present models of how people should live”
“modern day America” → “contemporary America”
“nolonger” → “no longer”
“realitive humanness” → “shared humanity”
“thier inadequacy to achieve” → “their failure to succeed”
“The parts of the new machine that need oiled and regulated.”  
  This sentence is incomplete. If you want a metaphor, explain it more clearly.

Spelling and mechanics
Please correct:
comercials → commercials
cen → century
americans → Americans
substitued → substituted
unsuccessfull → unsuccessful
thier → their

Also, avoid using & in formal academic writing. Write and instead.

Academic tone
Your ideas are appropriate for an academic essay, but some phrasing should become more formal and precise. For example:
instead of “buy more things,” you could write “increase consumption”
instead of “the unlucky,” you could write “those who are disadvantaged”

A possible revision of your opening
Here is one possible way to rewrite your paragraph more clearly:

> A dominant ideology in the United States suggests that success is available to everyone and that those who fail simply have not worked hard enough or followed society’s written and unwritten rules. Popular media reinforces this belief every day. Television programs and commercials present idealized models of success and proper behavior, while rarely acknowledging the structural inequalities that limit many people’s opportunities. Although the United States was founded on values such as equality and freedom, these ideals have never been fully realized in practice. In contemporary America, new technologies have increased the pace of life, encouraging people to work more and consume more while spending less time with family and community. As society becomes more individualistic, people are less likely to recognize their shared humanity and more likely to blame disadvantaged individuals for their lack of success.

Do not copy this exactly unless your instructor allows it, but use it as a model for sentence structure and clarity.

Revision priorities
When you revise, focus on these steps:
Write a clear thesis in one sentence.
Break long sentences into shorter, correct sentences.
Organize ideas in a logical sequence.
Add specific examples from media or society.
Proofread for spelling, punctuation, and formal style.

You have a thoughtful argument here. If you make the structure clearer and support your claims with examples, your essay will become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a strong personal voice and a clear concern about the social effects of technology, especially in the United States. You also use vivid language and personal examples, which makes the argument engaging. For revision, the main areas to improve are focus, argument structure, clarity, and sentence accuracy.

Main strength: strong voice and engagement
Your writing sounds thoughtful and passionate. Phrases such as “both the closest friend and yet the greatest Nemesis of our world” show ambition and rhetorical style. The essay also benefits from personal experience: your examples about education, language learning, and children’s lack of geographic knowledge help make the topic concrete.

Main problem: the argument is too broad and not fully focused
At the start, your topic is technology as a whole, but later the essay becomes mainly about:
American education
ignorance of geography
lack of foreign language learning
selfishness or isolation in U.S. culture

These are interesting ideas, but the connection to technology is not always clear. As a result, the reader may feel that the essay changes topic.

How to revise
Choose a more precise thesis. For example:
Technology can improve life, but in the United States it has also encouraged isolation and reduced young people’s interest in other cultures.

This gives you a clear line of argument:
Technology brings benefits.
However, it can also create social and educational problems.
These problems are visible in young Americans’ weak global awareness.

Organization
Right now, the essay reads more like a reflection than a structured argument. Try this structure:

Introduction
Introduce the topic.
Present a clear thesis.

Body paragraph 1: Benefits of technology
Use your examples:
travel
support for your family
medical progress

Body paragraph 2: Negative effects
Explain specifically how technology can be harmful:
creates comfort and self-sufficiency
reduces curiosity about the outside world
encourages passive consumption rather than learning

Body paragraph 3: Education and youth
Develop your U.S. examples:
weak geography education
limited language learning
anecdote about Spain/Michigan

Conclusion
Return to the main claim:
technology itself is not evil
the problem is how society uses it
education should teach global awareness alongside technological progress

Development of ideas
Some claims are interesting but need more explanation or evidence. For example:

“technology has become a large detriment to our youth”
“a great majority of Americans live in their own personal closets”
“the effort of the United States has caused our minds to be heard”

These statements are strong, but the reader needs clearer explanation. Ask yourself:
What exactly does technology do to young people?
How does it reduce cultural awareness?
Why is this connected to education?

Try to explain each point step by step.

Style and wording
Your style is ambitious, but sometimes it becomes unclear or overly dramatic. Academic argumentative writing usually works better with precise, controlled language.

Examples
“This thing, technology…” → sounds informal and awkward.
“greatest Nemesis” → too dramatic; also “nemesis” is usually not capitalized.
“personal closets” → vivid, but unclear.
“Science and technology have had effects on the U.S. that cannot be described” → if they cannot be described, then your argument cannot develop them.

Try to prefer clear meaning over dramatic effect.

Grammar and sentence issues
There are several sentence-level problems, especially with run-on sentences, punctuation, article use, and phrasing.

Examples and corrections
“Science and technology have allowed me to travel to this part of the world, they have provided…”  
  → This is a run-on sentence. Use a period or semicolon.  
  Better: “Science and technology have allowed me to travel to this part of the world. They have provided…”

“The use of the means humans have developed is wherein the greatest problems lie.”  
  → Grammatically awkward.  
  Better: “The greatest problems lie in how humans use the tools they have developed.”

“technology has become a large detriment to our youth”  
  → More natural: “technology has become highly detrimental to young people” or “technology has harmed young people in important ways.”

“because of these circumstances, that I mentioned before”  
  → Remove “that.”  
  Better: “because of these circumstances, which I mentioned before…”

“I had only one choice of languages- German.”  
  → Better punctuation: “I had only one language choice: German.”

“The effort of the United States has caused our minds to be heard…”  
  → This sentence is unclear and likely not what you mean. Rephrase completely.

Likely German-influenced issues
As a German speaker, you may naturally write long sentences with many linked ideas, but in English argumentative essays, shorter and clearer sentences are often stronger. Also watch for:
capitalization of nouns (not needed in English, except proper nouns)
hyphen/dash use
word order in complex sentences
choosing idiomatic phrases rather than literal translations

Final advice
For your next draft, focus on:
writing a clear thesis
connecting every paragraph directly to that thesis
explaining more clearly how technology causes the problems you describe
simplifying sentence structure
revising unclear or overly dramatic phrases

You have the basis of a compelling argument. With a narrower focus and clearer structure, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a consistent position: computers have become essential in modern life. The introduction and conclusion match well, and your main idea is easy to understand. You also organize the essay into separate paragraphs with a logical progression: everyday use, dependence, life changes, and conclusion. This is a good foundation.

Strengths
Clear focus: You stay on the topic throughout the essay.
Logical structure: The essay moves from general statements to specific areas like school, work, and home.
Relevant examples: You mention homes, schools, businesses, and job skills, which helps show the broad impact of computers.
Strong concluding idea: The point about people noticing computers most when they break down is effective.

Main areas to improve

Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction presents the topic, but the main argument could be stronger. Right now, “Computers have significantly changed people’s lives” is true, but still broad. Try to state how and why they changed life.

For example:
Stronger thesis: *Computers have transformed modern life by making work faster, improving access to information, and increasing people’s dependence on technology.*

This gives your essay a clearer argumentative direction.

Develop your paragraphs more fully
Many of your paragraphs make a point, but they need more explanation or evidence. Often you repeat the same idea—computers are useful, efficient, and common—without adding a new level of analysis.

For revision, try this paragraph pattern:
Topic sentence
Explanation
Example
Link back to your argument

For instance, in the paragraph about schools, you could explain not only that computers are “fun and exciting,” but also how they help students research information, practice skills, or prepare for future jobs.

Be careful with repetition
Several ideas appear many times:
computers are part of everyday life
computers are useful
people depend on computers

Instead of repeating these points, add new dimensions:
communication
online banking/shopping
healthcare
transportation
disadvantages of dependence

This will make the essay more persuasive and more interesting.

Improve word choice and grammar
There are several language errors, some of which may come from direct translation or from German-influenced sentence structure. Here are important corrections:

“For many people computers have become…”  
  Better: *For many people, computers have become…*  
  Add a comma after the introductory phrase.

“calculators as usually found”  
  Correct: *calculators are usually found*

“in a private home”  
  More natural: *in private homes* or *at home*

“speed-up”  
  As a verb, write: *speed up*  
  Example: *Computers speed up many laborious tasks.*

“laboreous”  
  Correct spelling: *laborious*

“are relied on greatly”  
  This is understandable but unnatural. Better:
  *People rely heavily on computers…*

“because it is fun and exciting”  
  Since the subject is plural, use: *because they are fun and exciting*  
  However, even better would be a more formal reason than “fun.”

“the invention of the calculator (the first computer)”  
  This is factually incorrect. A calculator is not the first computer. You should write:
  *since the invention of the computer*  
  or, if you want historical accuracy,
  *since the development of modern computers*

“to help everyday life run smoothly”  
  Better: *to help everyday life run more smoothly*

“When computers break down people are thrown back…”  
  Add a comma after the introductory clause:
  *When computers break down, people are thrown back…*

Use a more formal academic tone
The opening and closing rhetorical question creates interest, but in an academic essay, it can sound a little informal if overused. Since you use almost the same question in both the introduction and conclusion, consider changing one of them into a formal statement.

For example:
Instead of *“How could people live without the invention of the computer?”*
Write: *It is difficult to imagine modern life without computers.*

This sounds more academic and direct.

Organization suggestion for revision
You could revise the essay into this structure:

Introduction
   - General statement about computers in modern life
   - Clear thesis with 2–3 main reasons

Body Paragraph 1: Computers in daily life
   - Home, school, work, shopping
   - Why they are common

Body Paragraph 2: Benefits
   - Save time
   - Increase productivity
   - Improve access to information

Body Paragraph 3: Dependence and drawbacks
   - People rely on computers too much
   - Problems when systems fail

Conclusion
   - Restate thesis
   - End with a strong final thought

Example of a revised sentence
Original:
*People have come to depend on the function of a computer to help everyday life run smoothly.*

Improved:
*People have become so dependent on computers that many parts of everyday life cannot function smoothly without them.*

Final comment
This essay shows good basic control of structure and a clear understanding of the topic. Your next step is to make the argument more specific, reduce repetition, and improve grammar and word choice. With these changes, your essay will become more convincing and more academic in style.

Your essay has an ambitious and interesting goal: you connect Einstein’s theory of relativity to philosophy, human ideas of time, and global politics. This is a strong starting point for an argumentative essay because it shows original thinking. You also write with energy and conviction. To revise successfully, focus on three main areas: argument, accuracy, and language control.

Main strengths
Original topic and voice: You are not simply describing Einstein; you are trying to argue that relativity changed how humanity thinks.
Big conceptual connections: The links between science, philosophy, time, and war are intellectually interesting.
Clear concern with human consequences: Your discussion of atomic weapons gives the essay moral seriousness.

Main issue: unclear thesis and line of argument
At the moment, the essay moves between several claims:
relativity changed human philosophy,
humans do not yet fully understand its impact,
Einstein’s ideas were misused in atomic bombs,
humanity should move from fear to peace.

These are all possible arguments, but they need to be organized under one clear thesis.

Possible revised thesis
You could write something like:
> Einstein’s theory of relativity transformed modern thought by challenging traditional ideas of time, knowledge, and certainty; however, its wider cultural impact has been limited because humanity has focused more on its technological consequences, especially atomic weapons, than on its philosophical meaning.

This would give the essay a clearer direction.

Organization
Try a more structured paragraph plan:

Introduction
Introduce Einstein and relativity.
Present your thesis clearly.

Paragraph 1: Relativity and human thought
Explain briefly how relativity challenged fixed ideas of space and time.
Show why this was philosophically important.

Paragraph 2: Why the impact remains incomplete
Explain why these ideas are difficult for ordinary people to grasp.
Discuss social conditioning and everyday ideas of time.

Paragraph 3: Misuse and political consequences
Explain the connection between Einstein, nuclear science, and atomic weapons carefully.
Discuss fear, the Cold War, and global politics.

Conclusion
Return to your main argument.
End with your claim about the need to shift from fear to peace.

Right now, some transitions are abrupt. For example, the move from personal reflections on time to atomic bombs needs a stronger bridge.

Accuracy and content
Some statements need correction or clarification:

“Albert Einstien” → Albert Einstein
“his wife’s” theory: This is controversial and unsupported here. Unless you provide evidence and explain it, do not include it.
“With this theory also came the area of quantum mechanics.” This is historically inaccurate. Relativity and quantum mechanics are different developments in modern physics.
Atomic bombs and Einstein: Einstein did not “invent” the bomb. His work contributed indirectly to modern physics, and he signed a letter to Roosevelt, but the bomb was developed by many scientists. Be precise.
“The Socratic idea that all was known” is not accurate. Socrates is more associated with questioning knowledge than claiming all was known.
Stephen Hawkings → Stephen Hawking

If you make philosophical or historical claims, try to be more specific and careful.

Language and style
Your essay contains many strong phrases, but grammar and wording often make the meaning unclear.

Articles and possession
“one of histories most incredible minds” → “one of history’s most extraordinary minds”
“has volumes, volumes a literature” → “has generated volumes of literature”

Word choice
“point of departure” is understandable, but you repeat it too much. You could vary it with reference point, starting point, or frame of reference.
“indwell the topic” sounds unnatural in academic English. Try engage deeply with the topic or think through the concept.
“earthtime” is unclear. Use conventional human time, clock time, or the 24-hour day.

Sentence structure and punctuation
Avoid sentence fragments:
“The socratic idea that all was known.” → This is not a full sentence.
“And that it was only a matter of time…” → Also a fragment.

Avoid unnecessary capitalization:
“And” in the middle of a sentence should not be capitalized.
“Socratic” should be capitalized because it comes from a proper noun.

Be careful with semicolons:
“USA-USSR; with Japan and Germany as the ‘winners’.”
This is not a correct semicolon use. Try:
> The development of atomic weapons shaped global systems, including the rivalry between the USA and the USSR, while Japan and Germany emerged after the war in complex new roles.

Spelling corrections
Einstien → Einstein
histories → history’s
nostolgic → nostalgic
short comings → shortcomings
Hawkings → Hawking
utopic is possible, but utopian is more common

Tone and evidence
Your style is passionate, but some phrases are too poetic for an academic essay:
“The philosopher’s stone was pulverized into utopic and nostalgic dust.”

This is vivid, but it does not explain the argument clearly. In revision, balance style with explanation.

Also, your essay needs more evidence. Even in a short argumentative essay, you should support claims with examples, historical facts, or brief references to Einstein’s ideas.

Final advice
For revision, focus on these priorities:
Write one clear thesis.
Organize the essay so each paragraph supports that thesis.
Correct factual inaccuracies.
Simplify and clarify your language.
Replace poetic but vague phrases with precise explanation.

You have strong ideas and an individual voice. If you make the argument clearer and more accurate, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: shopping malls have harmed downtown Kalamazoo and especially the people who remain there. This is a strong argumentative topic, and your paper shows good awareness of history, class, transportation, and urban change. You also use Kalamazoo as a concrete example, which gives the essay focus. For revision, the main goals are to strengthen the organization, clarify the argument, and improve grammar/word choice.

Main strengths

Clear opinion: Your thesis is easy to understand: malls are “more of a detriment than a blessing.”
Interesting social argument: You connect malls to suburbanization, transportation, and inequality. This gives the essay depth.
Use of local example: Kalamazoo makes the argument more vivid and specific.

Main issues to revise

A. Make the thesis more specific
Right now, your thesis says malls are harmful, but it does not clearly say how. A stronger thesis should name your main reasons.

For example:
> Although shopping malls are convenient for many consumers, they have damaged downtown Kalamazoo by drawing businesses away from the city center, increasing social separation between rich and poor, and weakening neighborhoods that depend on local commerce.

This gives the reader a map of the essay.

B. Improve paragraph structure
Some paragraphs contain many ideas at once. Try to organize each paragraph around one main point:

Introduction + thesis
Historical development of Kalamazoo
How wealthier residents and businesses moved outward
Effects on poor and working-class residents
Possible exceptions (colleges, festivals)
Conclusion

Also, avoid very short, separate sentences such as:
> Everyone was near the central city - so everyone could use the central city.

This idea is good, but it should be integrated into the paragraph in a more formal way:
> Because most residents lived near the city center, both rich and poor had access to its services and businesses.

C. Keep an academic tone
Some sentences sound too conversational or emotional for an academic essay.

For example:
> Why should we, say those with cars, go downtown...

This rhetorical question could be rewritten more formally:
> Many car owners prefer malls because they can shop indoors in one location rather than travel between separate downtown stores.

Also:
> and still could be!

This is too informal as a sentence fragment. Instead:
> With sufficient investment, downtown could still become a thriving neighborhood again.

Language corrections

There are several grammar and vocabulary problems, many of which are common for German speakers writing in English.

Word choice
“utilize” → usually “use” is more natural.
“clientel” → “clientele”
“caf�s” → “cafés”
“flu from the inner cities” seems to be a typo; probably you mean “flight from the inner cities”

Grammar
“where the could keep a distance” → “where they could keep their distance”
“the more poorer people” → “poorer people” or “the poorer residents”
“Many larger factories and office building” → “Many large factories and office buildings”
“many business try” → “many businesses try”
“There neighborhoods” → “Their neighborhoods”

Article/preposition problems
“locate themselves farther away” sounds unnatural. Better:
  > Cities later expanded farther outward because of trucks and cars.
“move outwards” → “move outward” is more natural in formal writing.
“remain and stay in business” is repetitive. Use one:
  > remain in business

Clarity and logic
Some claims need more explanation or support. For example:

You say malls hurt downtown businesses. Explain exactly why: convenience, parking, concentration of stores, suburban consumer traffic, etc
You say the poor are most harmed. Give clearer reasons: fewer nearby stores, less investment, fewer jobs, decaying neighborhoods.
The paragraph about colleges and festivals is interesting, but you should explain how it fits your argument. Does it show that downtown can still attract people? If so, say that directly.

Suggested style improvements

Try to make sentences shorter and more precise. Here is one example of revision:

Original:
> This process has left the businesses in the inner cities, including Kalamazoo, without a stable clientel.

Revised:
> As wealthier residents moved to the suburbs, many downtown businesses in cities such as Kalamazoo lost their stable clientele.

This version is clearer, more concise, and more formal.

Final advice
When you revise, focus first on argument and structure, then on sentence-level grammar. Your essay already has a meaningful central idea. The next step is to present it more logically and formally.

Revision checklist
Write a more specific thesis.
Give each body paragraph one main idea.
Replace conversational phrases with academic language.
Correct grammar, spelling, and word choice.
Explain your examples more fully.
End with a conclusion that restates your argument clearly.

You have a strong foundation here. With clearer organization and more careful language, this could become a convincing and thoughtful argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear argument and a recognizable structure. You present a balanced view at first—television has positive potential but has mostly had negative effects—and this gives your essay a strong basis for argument. Your vocabulary is also fairly advanced, and the reader can understand your main point throughout. For revision, focus on making your thesis more precise, strengthening evidence and logic, and correcting grammar and word choice.

Strengths

Clear topic and position
The essay clearly argues that television has had a major influence on society.
You do not present television as purely good or bad; instead, you argue that its potential has been misused. This is a thoughtful claim.

Logical overall organization
Paragraph 1 introduces the topic and your main claim.
Paragraphs 2–3 explain positive possibilities and actual negative effects.
Paragraph 4 provides a conclusion.

Good range of ideas
You discuss education, culture, family life, laziness, and escape from reality. This gives your essay breadth.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis sharper
Your main claim is interesting, but it could be more specific. At the moment, the essay says television has changed society and often encourages escape, laziness, and family distance. Try to express this in one precise sentence at the end of the introduction.

For example:
“Although television can educate and broaden people’s understanding of the world, its dominant role in daily life has encouraged passivity, weakened family interaction, and turned entertainment into a form of escape.”

This would help guide the whole essay.

B. Support claims with clearer evidence
Many of your statements are strong, but they are very general:
“T.V. is a major cause of lack of togetherness…”
“The more time one spends watching TV, the less motivated he becomes…”

These claims need support. In an academic essay, you should explain why this is true or give examples. Even if you are not using outside sources, you can add more concrete reasoning:
How exactly does TV reduce family togetherness?
Are families together physically but not communicating?
What kinds of worthwhile activities are replaced by television?

Try developing each body paragraph with specific examples rather than broad statements.

C. Improve paragraph unity
Paragraph 2 contains several different ideas: educational programming, cultural exposure, war coverage, artistic expression, and escapism. Consider splitting or organizing it more clearly:
Television’s positive potential
How society actually uses it as escapism

This would make your argument easier to follow.

Language corrections

Here are some important sentence-level issues.

Grammar and word choice
“has potential to offer” → “has the potential to offer”
“than cannot be given by newspaper” → “than can be given by newspapers”
“resulted in an increase in laziness” → better: “encouraged passivity” or “contributed to laziness”
“00 years ago” → “100 years ago”
“integrety” → “integrity”
“dificulties” → “difficulties”
“has becomed” → “has become”

Article use
As a German speaker, you may sometimes omit or misuse articles:
“by newspaper” → “by newspapers” or “by the newspaper”
“has potential” → “has the potential”

Word form and natural phrasing
Some expressions are understandable but not idiomatic:
“world views” is better as “worldviews” or “views of the world”
“creative vein” sounds unusual here; better: “creative medium” or “creative outlet”
“staring at a box” is vivid, but slightly informal. That may be acceptable in argumentative writing, but if you want a more academic tone, say “passively watching a screen.”

Style and academic tone

Your tone is persuasive, but sometimes it becomes too absolute:
“most Americans”
“millions of Americans”
“major cause”

Be careful with overgeneralization unless you can prove it. You can make the language more academic by softening claims:
“for many Americans”
“can contribute to”
“is often seen as”

This makes your argument sound more credible.

Suggested revision priorities

Rewrite the thesis so it clearly names the main negative effects.
Reorganize paragraph 2 for better unity.
Add specific examples or explanations for your key claims.
Correct grammar, spelling, and article use.
Reduce overgeneralized statements and use more academic phrasing.

Example of a revised sentence

Original:
“Therefore, since the invention of television, society has becomed more informed but too lazy to think about this new information, and has distanced itself from family life.”

Revised:
“Therefore, although television has made society better informed, it has also encouraged passive consumption of information and weakened meaningful interaction within families.”

This version is clearer, more formal, and grammatically correct.

Overall, this is a strong draft with a clear central idea. The next step is to make your reasoning more precise and your language more polished.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0011.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that the polio vaccine was one of the most important inventions of the twentieth century because it reduced suffering and opened the way for later medical advances. This is a strong and interesting topic, and your writing shows genuine engagement with it. The next step is to make your argument more precise, organized, and formal.

Main strengths

Clear overall claim: You consistently present the polio vaccine as historically important.
Sense of significance: You communicate well that polio caused fear, death, and disability, and that the vaccine changed many lives.
Ambitious argument: You try to connect the vaccine not only to health, but also to broader social change.

Biggest revision priorities

A. Clarify and narrow your thesis
Your thesis is currently too broad and sometimes exaggerated. For example, “the greatest invention and discovery” and “inequivalent to any other invention” are hard to prove. In an argumentative essay, strong claims need strong evidence.

A more effective thesis would be more specific, such as:

Possible revision:  
*The polio vaccine was one of the most important medical achievements of the twentieth century because it dramatically reduced death and disability and helped build public confidence in modern vaccination.*

This keeps your main idea but makes it more academic and defensible.

B. Improve organization
Right now, the essay has an introduction and two body paragraphs, but the ideas within the paragraphs move around. Try to give each paragraph one main focus.

A clearer structure could be:

Introduction  
   - Background on disease and polio  
   - Thesis

Body paragraph 1: Human impact of polio  
   - Fear of the disease  
   - Death and paralysis  
   - How the vaccine reduced these effects

Body paragraph 2: Broader historical importance  
   - Public trust in vaccination  
   - Influence on later medical progress  
   - Why this makes the vaccine historically significant

Conclusion  
   - Restate thesis  
   - Emphasize long-term importance

Use evidence more carefully
Several points need support or more cautious wording:

The connection between the polio vaccine and the Baby Boom is not convincing as written. The Baby Boom is usually explained by social and economic factors after World War II, not mainly by one vaccine. If you keep this point, qualify it carefully.
Statements like “Every few years a new one comes along to normalize an overpopulated world” sound too sweeping and informal, and they are not appropriate for an academic argument.
“The polio vaccine stopped death in its path” is powerful, but too absolute. Vaccines reduced polio dramatically; they did not eliminate all death everywhere instantly.

Try to use measured academic language:
instead of “the greatest invention”, write “one of the most important medical advances”
instead of “stopped death in its path”, write “greatly reduced deaths and disabilities caused by polio”

Style and tone
Your essay sometimes sounds conversational rather than academic. Examples include:
“For sure”
“I’m sure”
“for each individual to decide”
“Things weren’t impossible any more”

In academic writing, avoid speaking directly from personal opinion unless the assignment asks for it. Replace personal phrases with objective ones.

For example:
Informal: “I’m sure in the past there were ‘booms’...”  
More academic: “Population increases had also occurred in earlier periods, especially after wars.”

Also avoid symbols like “&” in formal essays. Write “and.”

Grammar and language issues

Because your first language is German, pay special attention to sentence boundaries and word choice. Some useful corrections:

“For centuries before” → *For centuries*
“permanently mamed” → *permanently maimed*
“starting at the middle of the century” → *beginning in the middle of the century*
“future’s children” → *future generations* or *children of the future*
“20 year olds” → *twenty-year-olds*
“as a child” → *as children*
“than time” → *that time*
“a role of population control” → *a role in population control*
“The process of vaccination also opened new doors” → acceptable, but *The development of the vaccine also opened new possibilities* sounds more formal.

Also watch punctuation:
Add a comma after introductory words: “Unfortunately, this still occurs...”
Avoid repeated words: “never never”
Be careful with dashes and hyphens; some of your sentences are too long and should be separated.

Example of a stronger revised sentence
Original:  
*Perhaps the greatest invention and discovery is the polio vaccine, invented by Salk, which changed the future, and future's children.*

Revised:  
*Jonas Salk’s polio vaccine was one of the most important medical breakthroughs of the twentieth century because it transformed the lives of future generations.*

Final advice
For revision, focus on three things:

Make your thesis more precise and less absolute
Organize each paragraph around one clear idea
Use formal, evidence-based language

You already have a meaningful topic and a strong sense of its importance. With clearer structure and more careful phrasing, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and a sensible structure. You argue that the airplane changed people’s lives, and you organize the body into major areas: travel, business, entertainment, and international relations. This is a strong foundation for an argumentative essay because the reader can follow your ideas easily.

What is working well

Clear main idea  
   Your introduction states your opinion directly: the invention of the airplane has drastically changed people’s lives.

Logical organization  
   Each body paragraph focuses on one area of impact. This makes the essay easy to understand.

Relevant examples  
   You use examples such as travel from Michigan to California and international business. These help make your points concrete.

Main areas to improve

Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction gives the general topic, but your thesis could be sharper and more formal. Instead of listing many ideas loosely, give one sentence that previews your argument.

Possible revision:  
*The invention of the airplane has transformed modern life by reducing travel time, expanding international business and entertainment, and reshaping political relations between countries.*

This version is more academic and clearly introduces the essay’s main points.

Improve paragraph development
Some paragraphs make a good claim, but the explanation is sometimes too general. Try to use this pattern:

topic sentence → explanation → example → concluding sentence

For example, in the business paragraph, you say that countries can work together more easily, but you do not explain exactly how. You could mention business meetings, faster transport of personnel, or the growth of multinational companies.

Use a more formal academic style
Several expressions are too informal for an essay:
“As I’ve already said”
“I’m going to touch on”
“just gave a concert”
“definitely flourished”

Also, avoid & in formal writing. Write and.

Try to sound more objective and formal.

Examples:
*As I’ve already said* → *As mentioned above* or simply delete it
*I’m going to touch on* → *The final area to consider is*
*just gave a concert* → *recently gave a concert*

Correct grammar and word choice
There are many small language errors, some of which are likely influenced by German. Focus especially on these:

Verb forms
*have been significantly shorten* → have been significantly shortened
*than previously possibly* → than was previously possible
*the do* → they do

Word choice
*effected* is often incorrect here.  
  Use affected when you mean “influenced.”
  - *has been drastically effected* → has been drastically affected
*choosen* → chosen
*perspective countries* → respective countries
*musicans* → musicians
*Pearl Habor* → Pearl Harbor
*efficeintly* → efficiently

Sentence structure
Some sentences are too long or awkward. For example:

*Air transportation allows people to travel from place to place more quickly & efficeintly & overseas travel has been drastically effected.*

Better:  
*Air transportation allows people to travel more quickly and efficiently, and it has dramatically affected overseas travel.*

Strengthen transitions
Your essay is organized, but transitions between ideas can be smoother. Use linking phrases such as:
*In addition,*
*Furthermore,*
*For instance,*
*On the other hand,*
*As a result,*
*Therefore,*

These will help your argument flow more naturally.

Improve the conclusion
Your final sentence is unclear:
*The invention of the airplane has had many positive effects-, unfortunately, however, it was brought about some negative changes, also.*

Problems:
punctuation is incorrect
*it was brought about* is ungrammatical
*unfortunately* and *however* together are awkward

Possible revision:  
*In conclusion, the airplane has brought enormous benefits in travel, business, entertainment, and diplomacy, although it has also contributed to destructive developments such as warfare.*

This gives the essay a stronger ending.

Notes on punctuation and mechanics
Do not put spaces before commas or periods.
Avoid unnecessary parentheses: *(vacation), (politics)*. Integrate these words into the sentence instead.
Be careful with apostrophes:
  - *would've* is too informal; write would have
Write numbers consistently in formal style: *30 to 35 hours* is often better than *30-35 hours* in an essay.

A possible revision strategy
Rewrite the thesis so it clearly presents your argument.
Revise each body paragraph to include one main claim, explanation, and a specific example.
Correct informal language and replace & with and.
Proofread carefully for spelling and grammar, especially affected/effected, verb forms, and word choice.
Rewrite the conclusion so it summarizes your argument clearly.

Overall, this essay shows good ideas and a clear structure. The main work now is to improve language accuracy and make the style more academic. With careful revision, this can become a strong argumentative essay.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0013.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay presents a clear position: AIDS changed people’s lives not only medically, but also socially and psychologically. You also use examples, such as Ryan White, to support your argument. That gives your essay a strong starting point. For revision, focus on three main areas: accuracy of ideas, organization, and language control.

Strengths
Clear topic: You identify AIDS as a discovery/event that changed society.
Relevant examples: The Ryan White example is effective because it shows fear and discrimination in a concrete way.
Argumentative focus: You are not only describing AIDS; you are arguing that it changed attitudes, behavior, and relationships.

Main revision priorities

A. Be more precise and accurate
Some claims are too absolute or factually questionable, which weakens your argument.

Examples:
“it always leads to death” — This is too absolute. Historically, AIDS was often fatal, but “always” is too strong.
“there is no cure” — This is clearer, but you should connect it carefully to the time period if needed.
“casual sex … rarely occurs” — This is a very broad generalization and is probably not true.
“homosexual male … they probably have AIDS too” — This reflects prejudice, but your sentence should make clearer that this is a false stereotype.

How to improve: Use cautious academic language:
“was widely feared because it was often fatal”
“led many people to believe…”
“contributed to stigma against gay men”
“made some people more cautious about sexual behavior”

B. Strengthen organization and paragraph focus
Your essay has an introduction and body ideas, but the structure can be clearer. Right now, some points repeat fear/paranoia without a strong progression.

A possible structure:
Introduction: Present AIDS as a disease that changed society and explain your main argument.
Body paragraph 1: AIDS created fear and prejudice.
Body paragraph 2: AIDS changed public attitudes and behavior.
Conclusion: Summarize the social impact and importance of education.

Each paragraph should begin with a topic sentence that clearly states its main idea.

For example:
“One major effect of AIDS was the fear and discrimination it created.”
“In addition, the epidemic changed how many people thought about sex and personal responsibility.”

C. Improve grammar, word choice, and mechanics
There are many language errors, but most are fixable. The biggest issues are spelling, apostrophes, word forms, and sentence flow.

Spelling
Check these words:
peoples lives → *people’s lives*
awhile → *a while*
it’s presence → *its presence*
untill → *until*
devistating → *devastating*
definately → *definitely*
all time high → *all-time high*

Apostrophes: *its* vs. *it’s*
This is a common problem for German speakers and many native speakers too:
it’s = *it is*
its = possessive

So:
“it’s presence” should be “its presence”
“it’s consequences” should be “its consequences”

Sentence structure and punctuation
Some sentences are long or joined awkwardly.

Example:
“Also, when Ryan White, a boy who contracted AIDS through a blood transfusion was diagnosed, the people of his town and his school treated him like a complete outcast…”
  
This is understandable, but easier to read if revised:
“Another example is Ryan White, a boy who contracted AIDS through a blood transfusion. After he was diagnosed, people in his town and school treated him like an outcast…”

Word choice
A few expressions sound informal or imprecise for an academic essay:
“a complete outcast” → *an outcast*
“really made people think” → *forced many people to reconsider*
“breaking us apart” → *dividing society*

Style and academic tone
Try to reduce phrases like:
“I feel”
“really”
“I’m sure”

These make the argument sound more personal and less academic. Instead, use:
“This suggests that…”
“This development forced many people to…”
“Greater education may lead to further changes…”

Possible revised thesis
Your introduction would be stronger with a more specific thesis. For example:

“Although AIDS was first understood only recently, it has had a profound effect on society by creating fear and prejudice and by changing how many people think about sexuality, relationships, and personal responsibility.”

Final advice
When revising, focus first on content and structure, then on grammar and spelling. Your ideas are meaningful, but they need more precise language and better support. Avoid overgeneralizations, make your paragraphs more focused, and use a more formal tone.

You already have the basis of a strong essay. With revision, it can become much clearer and more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear central claim: nuclear weapons and atomic energy were among the most important forces shaping the twentieth century. You also show strong engagement with the emotional and political effects of the Cold War, and your conclusion returns well to the idea of hope and change. The piece has vivid language and ambition. For revision, focus on three main areas: argument development, sentence control, and word choice/grammar.

Strengths

Clear position: Your opinion is easy to identify from the introduction.
Historical awareness: You connect World War II, the Cold War, and post-Cold War change.
Strong voice: Phrases such as “fear began to replace nationalism” and “mental barriers are falling” are memorable and effective.
Good overall structure: Introduction → effects of nuclear weapons → recent changes → hopeful conclusion.

Main issues to revise

A. Make the argument more precise
Right now, the essay is persuasive in tone, but some claims are too broad or unsupported.

For example:
“The twentieth century has seen more wars than any other previous bloc of time.”
“bringing more change than our planet has seen since its genesis”
“spent uncountable trillions of dollars”

These statements sound powerful, but they may seem exaggerated. In academic argument, exaggeration weakens credibility. Try to replace very large claims with specific, supportable ones.

Possible revision strategy:
Explain *why* nuclear weapons were so significant:
  1. they changed military strategy,
  2. they created global fear,
  3. they shaped politics and public life,
  4. they now influence hopes for peace and peaceful technology.

This would make your essay more analytical and less general.

B. Improve paragraph unity
Your second paragraph contains many interesting ideas, but they are crowded together: missile technology, fear, American childhood memories, government spending, presidential administrations, and military culture. Try to make each paragraph focus on one main point.

For example:
Paragraph 1: introduction and thesis
Paragraph 2: nuclear weapons created fear and changed daily life
Paragraph 3: the end of the Cold War created new possibilities
Conclusion: hope for peaceful uses of atomic energy

C. Clarify meaning in several sentences
Some sentences are difficult to understand because of punctuation or phrasing. Shorter sentences will help.

For example:
> “Though advances in communication, transportation, and information sharing, the world as a body of people living in close proximity, has rapidly shrunk.”

Problems:
“Though” should probably be “Through”
the phrase in commas is awkward

A clearer version:
> “Through advances in communication, transportation, and information sharing, the world has rapidly become smaller and more interconnected.”

Language corrections

Here are some important corrections:

“bloc of time” → “period of time”
“Though advances” → “Through advances”
“the second of the world wars” → “the Second World War” or “World War II”
“the discovery and harness of atom” → “the discovery and harnessing of the atom”
“factors of change” → “factors of change” is understandable, but “agents of change” or “causes of change” sounds more natural
“As missile technology and nuclear fusion capacities increased” → probably “nuclear capabilities”; “fusion” is not the right word here unless you specifically mean fusion weapons
“anti-Christ” → “the Antichrist” if used as a religious reference
“secret cities for underground” → unclear; perhaps “underground shelters”
“In a planet” → “On a planet”
“cheif” → “chief”
“commandpost” → “command post”
“arrived a standstill” → “come to a standstill”
“mediums of strategy” → better “instruments of strategy” or “tools of war”

Grammar and punctuation

You often use commas where English would prefer either no comma or a full stop. This creates run-on or overloaded sentences.

Example:
> “From my experience as an American, I can remember associating communism with nuclear threat, Soviet citizens with anti-Christ, and bomb shelters, secret cities for underground, and weapons orbiting our planet, with daily, normal life.”

This sentence is too long and unclear. You could divide it:
> “As an American, I remember associating communism with nuclear threat. Soviet citizens seemed like enemies, and bomb shelters and weapons orbiting the planet became part of ordinary life.”

Also be careful with articles:
the atom
the Cold War
the military
a standstill

Style advice for a German-speaking writer

German academic writing often allows longer, more heavily layered sentences. In English argumentative essays, clarity usually improves when you:
use shorter sentences,
place the main subject and verb early,
avoid too many abstract nouns in one sentence,
prefer direct phrasing over poetic exaggeration.

Your essay has strong rhetoric, but revision should aim for clear argument first, style second.

Final suggestion

When revising, ask yourself:
What exactly is my thesis?
Does each paragraph support it with one clear idea?
Can I replace exaggerated claims with precise ones?
Can I shorten long sentences for clarity?

Overall, this essay shows strong ideas and genuine engagement with the topic. With clearer structure, more precise claims, and careful editing, it could become a much more convincing academic argument.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a logical general structure: it begins with the discovery of penicillin, moves to later developments in antibiotic synthesis, and ends with its broader impact. The tone is appropriately academic, and your vocabulary shows ambition. Below are the main areas to improve in revision.

Main argument and focus

At the moment, the essay reads more like a short historical overview than a strong argumentative essay. You describe what happened after the discovery of penicillin, but your central claim is not sharply stated.

What to improve
Add a thesis that makes an arguable point, for example:
*The discovery of penicillin not only transformed medical treatment but also reshaped the pharmaceutical industry and scientific research.*
*Penicillin’s importance lies not only in its medical benefits but also in the way it stimulated synthetic drug development and industrial expansion.*

Then make sure each paragraph supports that argument:
medical impact,
scientific/synthetic developments,
economic/industrial consequences.

Development of ideas

You mention several important points, but many are too general. The essay would be stronger if you explained them more specifically.

Examples
“many uses in stopping and preventing the spread of infections” → Which infections? Why was this important?
“other naturally-occurring antibiotics” → Give one or two examples if appropriate.
“changed the way people thought about taking medicine” → Explain how and why.

Be careful with claims that may be factually weak:
“It was now much easier to obtain antibiotics for simple illnesses, such as the common cold.”  
  This is problematic because the common cold is caused by viruses, and antibiotics do not treat viral infections. If you want to discuss overuse or misuse of antibiotics, you should say so clearly.

Organization and paragraphing

The overall progression is sensible, but some sentences feel disconnected because the paragraphs contain too many ideas at once.

Suggestions
Paragraph 1: discovery of penicillin and immediate medical importance.
Paragraph 2: development of synthesis and expansion of antibiotic research.
Paragraph 3: long-term effects on public health, industry, and ongoing research.

Use stronger topic sentences. For example:
*Penicillin revolutionized medicine by making bacterial infections far more treatable.*
*Its discovery also encouraged chemists to develop synthetic and semi-synthetic antibiotics.*
*Beyond medicine, penicillin helped create a powerful pharmaceutical industry.*

Style: avoid too much passive voice

You use passive constructions very often: “was discovered,” “were synthesized,” “were formed,” etc Passive voice is common in scientific writing, but overusing it makes your prose less direct.

Example revisions
“Penicillin was discovered to have many uses” → *Researchers soon found that penicillin could treat many infections.*
“Huge enterprises, such as Upjohn, were formed and grew” → *Companies such as Upjohn expanded rapidly.*

A mix of active and passive voice will sound more natural and stronger.

Grammar and word choice

There are several language issues that are typical but easy to revise.

Important corrections
“medicinal chemistry” → better: *medicine* or *medical science* in this context.
“As discoveries of new antibiotics decreased” → *As the discovery of new antibiotics slowed...*
“It was also attemped” → spelling: *attempted*
“increase of health of millions of people” → *improved the health of millions of people* or *increased life expectancy*
“research of their uses” → *research into their uses*
“a small bacteria” → *a small bacterium*  
  (“bacteria” is plural; “bacterium” is singular.)

Article/preposition choices
As a German speaker, pay attention to English article use:
“led to the increase of health” sounds unnatural; English prefers verbs like *improved health*.
“devoted to producing efficient synthesis routes for the production...” is repetitive. Try:  
  *devoted to developing efficient methods for synthesizing antibiotics.*

Clarity and concision

Some phrases are wordy or repetitive:
“discovery of a variety of other naturally-occurring antibiotics that could also be cultured ‘in vitro’ in a laboratory”  
  This can be simplified.
“more research, more companies, and more medical chemists”  
  Good parallelism, but perhaps too broad without examples.

Try shorter, clearer sentences where possible.

Conclusion

Your conclusion returns to the main topic effectively, but it could be more precise and forceful. Instead of ending with a poetic phrase alone, restate the main argument:
*In conclusion, the discovery of penicillin transformed modern medicine, inspired the development of new antibiotics, and helped build the modern pharmaceutical industry.*

Revision priorities

Write a clearer argumentative thesis.
Organize each paragraph around one specific supporting point.
Correct factual issues, especially the statement about the common cold.
Reduce passive voice and make sentences more direct.
Revise grammar and word choice, especially singular/plural forms and natural English phrasing.

You already have a strong foundation and a serious academic tone. With a clearer argument and more precise language, this essay could become much more persuasive and polished.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic, a recognizable argumentative structure, and a strong sense of purpose. You clearly explain that television affected entertainment, communication, and family life. These three main points give your essay good organization. For revision, focus on three areas: thesis and argument, grammar and word choice, and style/formality.

Strengths

Logical organization: Each body paragraph develops a separate point.
Balanced argument: You acknowledge both positive and negative effects, which makes the essay more thoughtful.
Strong conclusion: The ending returns to the main claim and gives the essay a sense of closure.

Main revision priorities

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction presents the topic well, but the thesis could be stronger and more direct. At the moment, the essay sounds partly descriptive and partly argumentative. Decide exactly what you want to argue.

For example, instead of:
“This invention not only introduced a new form of entertainment, it has enhanced communication and has altered family life and values.”

You could write:
“The invention of television was one of the most important developments of the twentieth century because it transformed entertainment, improved access to information, and reshaped family relationships and values.”

This version is more specific and clearly argumentative.

B. Develop analysis more deeply
You often make interesting claims, but some need more explanation. For instance:
Why is television better than radio?
In what ways does it improve communication?
How exactly does it strengthen or weaken family relationships?

Try to add one or two more sentences of explanation after each major claim. The essay will become more persuasive if you explain why each effect matters.

Language and grammar issues

There are several recurring errors. These are common for German-speaking learners, so they are very fixable.

A. Word choice / spelling
Please correct these:
their → there
20 century → 20th century
come in to → come into
way of live → way of life
programing → programming
yonkies → probably “junkies” or better: “become addicted to it”
communicating → communication
half way → halfway
news forms → new forms
the become → they become

B. Subject-verb agreement
Examples:
“the problem with television is people become…”  
  Better: “the problem with television is that people become…”
“The types of programs is another issue.”  
  Better: “The types of programs are another issue.”

C. Articles and phrasing
English often requires articles where German usage differs.
“Since the middle of the century the television has come into our homes”  
  Better: “Since the middle of the century, television has entered our homes…”
In general, use “television” rather than “the television” when speaking about the medium in general.

D. Repetition
You repeat “T.V.” and “communication/communicating” many times. Try varying your vocabulary:
television / TV / the medium / broadcasting
communication / exchange of information / access to news

Style and academic tone

This essay should sound more formal.

Avoid informal symbols
Replace:
& with and
T.V. with TV or television

Avoid vague or awkward expressions
“it is anyone’s guess if it will ever be resolved” is a bit conversational.
Try: “Whether this debate will ever be resolved remains uncertain.”

Use smoother transitions
Your structure is good, but transitions can be more formal:

In addition, it became an important source of information.
Finally, it influenced family interaction and values.

Sentence-level improvements

A few sentences could be rewritten for clarity:

Original:

Improved:

Original:

Improved:
“This form of communication has helped warn people about natural and social dangers and has increased awareness of global events.”

Final advice for revision

When revising, try this order:
Rewrite the thesis so your argument is sharper.
Expand each body paragraph with more explanation and examples.
Correct grammar and spelling, especially “there/their,” plural verbs, and article use.
Make the tone more academic by removing slang and symbols.

You already have a solid foundation: a clear structure, relevant ideas, and a consistent focus. With more precise language and deeper analysis, this can become a strong argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a recognizable argument: modern inventions such as the washing machine make life easier, but they may also contribute to laziness and reduced physical activity. This is a good basis for an argumentative essay because it presents both benefits and drawbacks. Your writing also has some lively, humorous images, such as people “clutching the machine tightly to their chests.” That shows creativity and gives the essay personality.

Main strengths
Clear central topic: the washing machine as a symbol of modern convenience.
Engaging tone: the essay is vivid and sometimes funny.
Attempt at argumentation: you do not only describe; you also question whether convenience is always positive.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis clearer
At the moment, your main claim appears quite late and is not stated very precisely. A stronger argumentative essay needs a clear thesis near the beginning.

For example, your main idea could be:
“Although washing machines save time and improve daily life, they also illustrate how modern technology can reduce physical activity and increase dependence on convenience.”

This gives the reader a clear map of your argument.

B. Improve paragraph structure
Right now, the essay reads more like a series of reflections than a fully developed argument. Try to give each paragraph one clear purpose:

Introduction – introduce the topic and thesis.
Benefits of washing machines – save time, reduce physical effort, improve hygiene.
Dependence on technology – people feel helpless without such devices.
Negative consequences – less movement, convenience culture, possible health effects.
Conclusion – restate the argument in a balanced way.

This will make your reasoning easier to follow.

C. Use stronger evidence and avoid overgeneralization
Some statements are exaggerated, for example:
“millions and millions of lives were made simpler”
“panic in most teenagers, students, & adults alike”
“many people might be seen furiously running about in the streets”

These examples are humorous, but in an academic argumentative essay they weaken credibility if there is no evidence. Try to replace exaggeration with more measured claims:
“Many people rely heavily on washing machines in daily life.”
“Without such appliances, household tasks would require much more time and effort.”

Also, your last sentence about Weight Watchers is interesting, but it needs support. If you want to connect appliances to obesity or inactivity, explain the reasoning more carefully and, ideally, use evidence.

D. Tone and register
The essay sometimes sounds too informal for academic writing. Avoid:
rhetorical questions used too often
“&” instead of “and”
multiple dots (“....”)
dramatic phrasing such as “devastating”

A more formal tone will strengthen your argument.

Language and grammar issues

A. Word choice and spelling
There are several spelling mistakes:
continously → continuously
necesity → necessity
with out → without
What ever → Whatever
handclothes washing → better: washing clothes by hand

B. Formal alternatives
man / modern man: This sounds old-fashioned and not inclusive. Better:
  - human beings
  - people
  - modern society
housewives: This may sound outdated. Better:
  - many people
  - many households
  - people responsible for housework

C. Pronouns
“Give an average adult a washing board… and tell him/her…” is awkward. In formal modern English, use:
them as a singular generic pronoun  
  - “Give an average adult a washboard and soap and tell them to do a load of laundry…”

D. Vocabulary accuracy
washing board → washboard
fire is not really a “discovery”; better would be the use of fire or the discovery of how to control fire

Style notes especially relevant for a German-speaking writer
German writing often allows longer build-up before the main point, but in English argumentative writing, readers usually expect the thesis early. Also, English academic style often prefers direct, explicit topic sentences. So instead of circling around the point, state it clearly and then support it.

Example of a stronger revised opening
Here is one possible revision of your introduction:

“Since ancient times, people have invented tools to make life easier. One modern example is the washing machine, which has greatly reduced the time and effort required for household work. However, although this invention offers clear practical benefits, it also reflects a broader dependence on technology that may reduce physical activity and increase reliance on convenience.”

This version is more formal, clearer, and more argumentative.

Final advice
When revising, focus on these priorities:
Write a clear thesis in the introduction.
Organize the essay into logical paragraphs.
Replace exaggeration with precise, supportable claims.
Use a more formal academic style.
Correct spelling and awkward phrasing.

You have an interesting core idea and a lively voice. With clearer structure and a more formal argument, this could become a strong essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay is lively, personal, and easy to follow. You clearly have a strong opinion, and the examples make your argument memorable. The informal voice (“I cannot live without!”) gives the essay energy, and the organization is mostly clear: you move from food storage to smells, then to travel and other practical uses. This gives the reader a good sense of why you value the invention.

What is working well

Clear position
You answer the prompt directly and stay focused on one invention: the zip-lock bag. That makes the essay coherent.

Good use of examples
The sandwich-in-the-locker example is especially effective because it is specific and humorous. It helps the reader understand your point rather than just reading general claims.

Strong sense of audience
Your essay sounds natural and conversational. The rhetorical question at the end works well to reinforce your conclusion.

Main areas to improve

Make the essay sound more academic
At the moment, the style is very informal. In an argumentative essay, a personal tone is acceptable, but some expressions are too conversational:
“It is funny that we are writing on this subject”
“I’m going to have to agree”
“A third solution would be to bag everything!”
“onlies???”

Try to keep your engaging voice, but use more formal wording. For example:

Informal: “It is funny that we are writing on this subject…”
More academic: “This topic is interesting to me because it recently came up in a discussion at home.”

Also remove side comments like “onlies???”, which weaken the authority of the essay.

Strengthen the thesis
Your introduction gives your opinion, but the main argument could be clearer. A stronger thesis should state why this invention is so valuable.

For example:
“The zip-lock bag is one of the most useful modern inventions because it preserves food, prevents odors and spills, and serves many practical purposes in everyday life.”

This gives the essay a clearer structure from the start.

Improve paragraph unity
Some body paragraphs mix several ideas together. For instance, the long paragraph about travel includes shampoo, Q-tips, pantyhose, earrings, shaving cream, and bathing suits. These examples are useful, but the paragraph becomes a list.

Try grouping ideas more clearly:
Paragraph 1: keeping food fresh
Paragraph 2: controlling odors
Paragraph 3: travel and household uses

Then use topic sentences that match those categories.

Work on grammar and word choice
There are several small language errors, many of which are typical for non-native writers. Here are some important ones:

“Anything can be put in zip-lock bag”  
  → “Anything can be put in a zip-lock bag”  
  You need an article.

“bring to school to lunch”  
  → “bring to school for lunch”

“foods… to which they are near”  
  → “foods near them” or “foods stored nearby”  
  Your version is understandable, but unnatural in English.

“these baggies are even better”  
  “Baggies” is very informal. Better: “these bags are especially useful.”

“Gallon size zip-lock bags, solve this problem.”  
  Remove the comma: “Gallon-size zip-lock bags solve this problem.”

“the earing”  
  → “the earring”

“the bathing suit that's too wet to pack”  
  Fine in speech, but more formal would be: “a wet bathing suit that would otherwise make other items damp.”

“nor take up space”  
  Better parallel form: “does not weigh much or take up much space.”

Check punctuation carefully
There are a few punctuation problems:
double punctuation: “locker. . Finally”
unnecessary comma: “Gallon size zip-lock bags, solve this problem.”
missing commas in lists
extra spaces before punctuation

These are small issues, but correcting them will make the essay look much more polished.

Suggested revision strategy

Rewrite the introduction with a clearer thesis.
Give each body paragraph one main idea.
Replace very informal expressions with more academic ones.
Edit for articles, prepositions, and punctuation.
Strengthen the conclusion by restating the main reasons, not only the final opinion.

Example of a stronger opening

“One of the most useful inventions in everyday life is the zip-lock bag. Although it is simple and inexpensive, it has many practical advantages. It preserves food, prevents odors and spills, and is helpful for storage both at home and while traveling.”

This version keeps your idea but presents it in a more formal argumentative style.

Final comment

This is an enjoyable essay with a clear central idea and strong real-life examples. Your biggest task now is not to change the content, but to make the writing more formal, organized, and grammatically polished. You already have good material; revision will help your argument sound more convincing and academic.

Your essay has a clear position and a logical overall structure.   - Example: databases, digital storage, search engines, online libraries.

  - Example: education, banking, medicine, entertainment.

Improve paragraph development

Be careful with overstatement
Some claims are too absolute:
“every human being in the developed world will have their own PC”
“small digital assistants that will replace the printed page”
“productivity ... has increased ten-fold”

In an argumentative essay, very strong claims should be supported by evidence. If you do not have evidence, soften the language:
“many people”
“is likely to”
“has increased significantly”

This makes your writing sound more credible.

Grammar and word choice corrections
There are several language issues that you should revise.

Spelling
occured → occurred

Word form / collocation
With the advancement in technology → With advances in technology / With technological advances
the way data and information is dealt with → the way data and information are handled

Number / article
the desktop PC has evolved → better: desktop PCs have evolved or the personal computer has evolved
their predecessors is correct, but check spacing: their predecessors

Agreement / pronouns
every human being ... will have their own PC  
  In modern English, singular they/their is common and acceptable, but in formal writing you may prefer:
  - will have his or her own PC, or better,
  - will own a personal computer

Ordinal number
2st century → 21st century

Punctuation
You generally use punctuation well, but there are places where sentence flow can improve. For example:

  → remove the unnecessary comma:  
  The personal computer has and will continue to play a major role in our lives.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: AIDS changed society’s attitudes toward sexuality, mortality, and public health, and you argue that education and compassion are more important than fear and discrimination. This is a strong and meaningful topic, and your essay shows engagement with it. Below are suggestions to help you revise.

Main strength: clear purpose
Your central idea is understandable throughout the essay. You show:
how public reactions to AIDS changed over time,
how stigma affected different groups,
why education matters,
and why people with AIDS deserve respect.

This gives your essay a clear argumentative direction.

Thesis and focus
Your opening sentence needs revision:

> “One of the most important discoveries of the 20th Century would have to be AIDS.”

This is inaccurate in meaning. AIDS was not a “discovery” in the positive sense; it is a disease that was identified. Also, your essay is not really about the medical discovery itself, but about society’s response to AIDS.

A better thesis might be:
The AIDS crisis changed public attitudes toward sexuality, health, and mortality, while also exposing prejudice and the need for better public education.
Although AIDS first produced fear and discrimination, it ultimately forced society to confront ignorance, improve education, and show greater compassion for those affected.

This would make your argument more precise from the beginning.

Organization
Your essay generally follows a logical order: early reactions, public awareness, and then your conclusion. However, the argument would be stronger if each paragraph had one clear main point.

A possible structure:
Introduction: present thesis about AIDS changing society.
Paragraph 1: early public reaction and stigma toward gay men and IV drug users.
Paragraph 2: growing awareness when children such as Ryan White became public cases.
Paragraph 3: the importance of sex education, prevention, and realistic public health policy.
Conclusion: need for acceptance, respect, and continued education.

Right now, some ideas are mixed together. For example, the Ryan White discussion moves quickly into condoms in schools. Add transitions so the reader can follow your logic more easily.

Use of evidence and tone
You make strong claims, but many need support or more careful wording. For example:
“no one really cared”
“Children are becoming sexually active at the age of eleven”
“Most people are more careful about who they sleep with”

These are broad statements. In academic writing, it is better to avoid absolute claims unless you can support them with evidence. Try softer, more precise language:
many people were indifferent
some policymakers and members of the public did not respond with urgency
public awareness increased safer-sex practices for some people

Also, your tone is sometimes persuasive and emotional, which can be effective, but academic essays usually sound more balanced and formal.

Historical and factual precision
Be careful with terms:
Use HIV/AIDS more accurately if possible. AIDS is the advanced stage of HIV infection.
“contract AIDS” is commonly understood, but “contract HIV” may be more medically precise.
“effect” should be affect in “they thought AIDS would never affect them.”

Grammar and style
You communicate clearly overall, but several language issues should be corrected.

Word choice
effect → affect
t.v. → TV or television
& → use and in formal writing
80's → 1980s or ’80s
twelve year old → twelve-year-old

Sentence problems
This is a fragment:
> “Even if a cure is never found.”

It should be connected to the next sentence:
Even if a cure is never found, AIDS victims should be accepted and given the respect they deserve.

Punctuation
Avoid double spaces and unusual punctuation:
“however, with the awareness also came discrimination” does not need a semicolon before it.
Hyphen use should be more standard: widely publicized, safe sex, sex education.

Style suggestions for a more academic register
Some phrases sound conversational:
“How wrong they were.”
“It’s too bad that it takes a superstar…”

You can revise these into a more formal style:
This assumption proved dangerously false.
It is unfortunate that public attention often increased only when famous individuals such as Magic Johnson disclosed their diagnosis.

A few sentence-level revisions
Examples of possible revision:

Original:
> “Once children became infected with AIDS, society began to care.”

Revised:
> Public concern increased significantly when children were publicly identified as victims of HIV/AIDS.

Original:
> “Thus began the campaign to educate the public on how one contracts AIDS.”

Revised:
> This led to broader public education campaigns about how HIV is transmitted.

Original:
> “AIDS victims should be accepted & given the respect that they deserve.”

Revised:
> People living with HIV/AIDS should be treated with dignity, compassion, and respect.

Final advice
For revision, focus on these priorities:
Write a more accurate and arguable thesis.
Make each paragraph center on one main idea.
Replace generalizations with more precise language.
Improve formal style and grammar.
Correct punctuation, spelling, and word choice.

You have a solid foundation and an important argument. With a more academic tone and clearer structure, this essay can become much stronger.

Your essay has a clear personal voice and a strong central claim: among twentieth-century inventions, medical inventions have improved people’s lives most. That is an interesting argument, and your opening shows real engagement with the topic. You also try to move beyond a simple “technology is good” position, which gives your essay ambition. This is easier to support than saying that medical inventions are the only positive ones.

antibiotics (penicillin)

You should also support your negative claims about other inventions. Right now, statements such as “we invent better weapons and better economic systems geared for killing and depriving other people” are too general. Give examples or soften the claim.

Body paragraph 1: explain why many inventions have harmed nature, with examples  
Body paragraph 2: explain how inventions can be used to dominate other people, with examples  
Body paragraph 3: explain why medical inventions are different, with several examples  
Conclusion: restate the argument and show why it matters

At present, the essay ends too quickly. The final paragraph should be expanded.
