Important corrections
occured → occurred
affect (noun) → effect  
  “have had a negative effect on our lives”
truely → truly
0the century → probably 20th century

our fellow man → this sounds old-fashioned; better: other people or fellow human beings

Also, some claims sound too absolute:
“cannot be used to dominate nature or other people”
This is not fully convincing; medical technology can also be connected to power and inequality. A more careful phrasing would strengthen your credibility:
“are primarily intended to preserve life rather than to control or destroy.”

Likely influence of German on your English
A few patterns may come from German:
Overly abstract phrasing: English academic writing usually prefers a balance of ideas and concrete examples.
Long philosophical sentences: these can be effective, but in English argument essays, shorter and clearer sentences often work better.
Capitalization/punctuation spacing: be careful with commas and spacing.

Suggested revised thesis
Here is one possible revision:
> Although many twentieth-century inventions have been used to exploit nature or increase human conflict, medical inventions such as antibiotics, vaccines, and diagnostic technologies have improved human life more consistently than any other form of invention.

This keeps your original idea but makes it more arguable and academic.

Final advice
When revising, ask yourself:
What exactly am I claiming?
What examples prove it?
Have I explained why those examples matter?
Have I avoided exaggeration?

You have the basis for a thoughtful essay. The next step is to turn your interesting ideas into a more persuasive academic argument by adding evidence, organizing your paragraphs more fully, and editing carefully for grammar and word choice.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and an arguable position: the satellite dish has had a major impact on communication, politics, business, and entertainment, with both positive and negative effects. This is a good basis for an argumentative essay. To revise successfully, focus on three main areas: thesis and argument, organization, and language accuracy/style.

Thesis and argument

At the moment, your essay reads more like a list of effects than a fully developed argument. You mention politics, business, MTV, cable TV, intelligence, and military programs, but the central claim is not precise enough.

Your final paragraph suggests the real argument: the satellite dish has transformed modern life in both beneficial and harmful ways. This idea should appear clearly in the introduction as a thesis.

This would give your essay a clear line of argument.

Also, some claims need more support or should be made more carefully. For example:

Faster international communication
Use by companies and investors
Importance for trade and financial information

MTV and global media culture
Growth of the entertainment industry
Cultural influence of satellite broadcasting

Information overload
Passive television consumption
Possible misuse in military or political systems

Restate argument
Give balanced final judgment

Right now, some paragraphs are too short and underdeveloped. For example, the paragraph about business is only two sentences and needs explanation. Try to develop each paragraph with topic sentence + example + explanation.

There are many useful ideas here, but grammar and word choice sometimes make the meaning unclear. As a German speaker, you may want to pay special attention to articles, apostrophes, capitalization, prepositions, and sentence structure.

Important corrections

“recieve” → “receive”

“that nights news” → “that night’s news”

“daily bases” → “daily basis”

“affect” → “effect” in “have had a strong effect”

“millionares” → “millionaires”

“people lives” → “people’s lives”

Capitalization

satellite dish not Satellite dish
information age unless part of a title
Panasonic should be capitalized correctly
country instead of ctry in formal writing

Formal style

“last 0 years”

Strengths

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and a strong central idea: television has changed people’s lives by shaping culture, commercializing daily life, and accelerating the spread of information. This is a solid argumentative structure, and your examples show good awareness of American culture and media history. The essay is interesting and readable. For revision, focus on improving accuracy, organization, and academic style.

Strengths

Clear thesis: You identify three major effects of television early in the introduction.
Logical paragraphing: Each body paragraph discusses one effect.
Concrete examples: References to TV families, advertising, and the Vietnam War help support your claims.
Strong concluding idea: Your final warning about television as both beneficial and manipulative is effective.

Main revision priorities

A. Clarify and strengthen the thesis
Your introduction is interesting, but the first sentence contains a major error:

“The 0th Century Invention” → should be “The 20th-century invention”

Also, your thesis would sound stronger if it were more direct and formal. For example:

> Television is one of the most influential inventions of the twentieth century because it has promoted mass culture, increased commercial influence on daily life, and transformed how quickly people receive information.

This version is clearer and more academic.

B. Improve argument precision
Some claims are too broad or unsupported. For example:

“We all received the same information.”
“To white people, Ghetto life is slightly less of a mystery.”

These statements generalize too much and may sound simplistic or insensitive. Try to qualify your claims:

“Many Americans were exposed to similar images and values through television.”
“Television also introduced some viewers to social realities outside their own communities, although these portrayals were often exaggerated or stereotypical.”

This makes your argument more balanced and persuasive.

C. Develop analysis, not just description
At times, you describe television’s effects but do not fully explain them. For instance, in the paragraph on advertising, you mention that commercials became an art form, but the paragraph is short and underdeveloped. Add analysis such as:

How advertising shapes desires and consumer behavior
How television links identity and happiness to products
Why this matters socially or politically

Each body paragraph should not only present an effect but also explain why it matters.

Organization and coherence

Your overall structure is good, but transitions can be improved. Add clearer topic sentences and links between ideas. For example:

Paragraph 2: explain that shared television programs helped create a more uniform national culture.
Paragraph 3: connect this cultural influence to consumer influence.
Paragraph 4: show that television affects not only what people buy, but also how they understand world events.

Also, your conclusion could summarize the three main points more explicitly before ending with your warning.

Language and style

A. Use formal academic English
Avoid informal abbreviations and symbols:

& → and
w/ → with
w/o → without
N.Y. → New York

Also avoid quotation marks when they are unnecessary, for example around “ideal” or “must-have”, unless you are analyzing the terms.

B. Correct word choice and spelling
Some errors interfere with clarity:

grupes → groups
breath → breadth
lead → led
peoples ability → people’s ability
livingrooms → living rooms
vietnam → Vietnam
inhance → enhance
The television a powerful and dangerous tool. → Television is a powerful and dangerous tool.

C. Punctuation and sentence control
You have several run-on sentences and punctuation problems. For example:

> “Today, we know within seconds, or in the case of the space-shuttle challenger explosion, we know live-immediately.”

This could be revised to:

> Today, information reaches us within seconds. In some cases, such as the Space Shuttle Challenger explosion, audiences witnessed events live.

This is clearer and more grammatically controlled.

D. Capitalization
Be careful with proper nouns and national terms:

united States → United States
space-shuttle challenger → Space Shuttle Challenger
vietnam war → Vietnam War

Content suggestions

You could make the essay stronger by acknowledging complexity. For example:

Television can spread shared cultural norms, but it can also reinforce stereotypes.

Correct grammar, spelling, and punctuation.
Replace informal expressions with formal academic language.
Rewrite the thesis more clearly.

You already have a strong basic argument and good examples. With clearer language and more precise analysis, this essay could become much more persuasive and polished.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear strength: it captures the emotional atmosphere of November 9, 1989 very effectively. The opening is vivid and engaging, and your personal connection gives the topic energy and sincerity. You also show a strong sense of the historical importance of the fall of the Berlin Wall. For revision, focus mainly on clarifying your argument, improving organization, and correcting grammar and word choice.

Argument and thesis

At the moment, your essay reads partly as a personal reflection and partly as an argument. Your main claim seems to be:

The fall of the Berlin Wall was a major catalyst in ending communism in Eastern Europe and the Cold War.

This is a strong arguable thesis, but it appears clearly only near the end. In revision, state it earlier, ideally at the end of the introduction. That will help the reader understand the purpose of the essay from the start.

For example, you could end the first paragraph with something like:

“The fall of the Berlin Wall was not only a symbolic event; it also became a catalyst for political change across Eastern Europe and contributed significantly to the end of the Cold War.”

Organization

Your essay has three paragraphs, but the structure could be more logical.

A clearer structure might be:

Introduction  
   Describe the day briefly and present your thesis.

Body paragraph 1: Symbolic importance  
   Explain why the wall mattered so much emotionally and politically.

Body paragraph 2: Effects on Eastern Europe  
   Discuss how events in Berlin encouraged change in other countries.

Body paragraph 3: Historical significance  
   Explain why this event contributed to the end of the Cold War, while acknowledging that other factors also mattered.

Conclusion  
   Restate the argument and reflect on why the day is still remembered.

Right now, some ideas appear suddenly, especially the anecdote about your classmate’s father. It is interesting, but you should connect it more directly to your argument. Explain how this example illustrates the oppression people experienced under communist regimes.

Style and tone

Your opening is powerful, but there are too many dramatic punctuation marks and pauses. The repeated dashes make the essay harder to read. In formal academic writing, use standard sentence structure and punctuation more consistently.

For example:

Original:  
“The world watched in anticipation -- We were mesmirized by the images of the TV...”

Better:  
“The world watched in anticipation, mesmerized by the images on television.”

Also, expressions like “our winning of the Cold War” sound awkward and politically loaded. If you use a phrase associated with President Bush, explain it carefully or rephrase more neutrally.

Grammar and language issues

There are several language errors, some of which may come from German influence. Below are important ones to correct:

Spelling
mesmirized → mesmerized
Tchekoslovaquia → Czechoslovakia
Roumania → Romania

Word form / article use
“one of those historic moment” → “one of those historic moments”
“the events of Berlin Wall” → “the events surrounding the Berlin Wall” or “the fall of the Berlin Wall”
“catalyst force” → “catalyst” or “catalytic force”

Grammar
“the day that the Wall fell in Berlin-”  
  Better: “the day the Berlin Wall fell.”
“the start of what our now as-President Bush likes to refer to”  
  Better: “the start of what President Bush now refers to”
“It was the event that gave the people of the Eastern Bloc countries the hope”  
  Better: “It was the event that gave people in the Eastern Bloc hope”
“I had asked him once if he hadn't been scared”  
  Better: “I once asked him whether he had been scared”
“Nothing could compare to the way he had been forced to live his life there”  
  This is understandable, but smoother would be: “Nothing, he said, was worse than the life he had been forced to live there.”

Tense consistency
You move between past and present in ways that are sometimes confusing. Since you are reflecting on past events from a later moment, be careful to keep the timeline clear.

Historical precision

Be careful with historical claims. For example, saying that the fall of the wall led to the end of communism everywhere may be too broad. In some countries, political change had already begun before November 1989. A stronger argument would acknowledge complexity:

“Although change was already underway in parts of Eastern Europe, the fall of the Berlin Wall became the most powerful symbol of communism’s collapse and encouraged further transformation.”

This makes your essay more convincing because it sounds balanced, not simplistic.

Final advice

When revising, try to:
state your thesis earlier and more clearly
reduce emotional repetition and excessive dashes
connect personal examples directly to your argument
improve grammar, spelling, and punctuation
make your historical claims more precise

Overall, this is a passionate and memorable essay with a strong sense of historical importance. With clearer structure and more formal language, it could become a much more persuasive argumentative piece.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and a recognizable argumentative structure. You argue that television is the most important invention of the twentieth century, and you support this by comparing it with earlier media such as radio and newspapers. The essay is easy to follow overall, and your conclusion tries to add complexity by noting both the benefits and the dangers of television. That is a strong basis for revision.

Main strengths

Clear central claim  
Your thesis is understandable: television changed modern life more than other inventions because it combined information and images and connected people across distances.

Logical organization  
The essay moves in a sensible order:
introduction to twentieth-century inventions,
background on radio/newspapers,
explanation of how television changed communication,
examples of its social importance,
conclusion with a warning about its power.

Ambitious vocabulary and tone  
You aim for a formal, persuasive style, which is appropriate for an argumentative essay. Phrases such as “the advent of the television” and “the power to reach so many” show good rhetorical intention.

---

Most important areas to revise

A. Make the thesis more precise
Right now, your thesis is somewhat broad and repetitive. Try to state exactly why television stands out.

For example, instead of:
> “Perhaps one does stand out a little more among its competition, however; that is the television.”

you could write:
> “Among twentieth-century inventions, television had the greatest impact because it transformed how people receive news, experience entertainment, and connect with events around the world.”

This gives the reader a clearer roadmap.

B. Strengthen the argument with more specific evidence
Your points are interesting, but many claims remain general. An argumentative essay becomes stronger when it includes concrete examples.

For instance, you mention that television allows people to see world events directly. You could support this with examples such as:
political speeches,
the moon landing,
Olympic events,
wars or disasters shown live.

Specific examples would make your argument more convincing.

C. Improve paragraph unity
Some paragraphs contain several ideas at once. Try to give each paragraph one main point:
Paragraph 1: introduction + thesis
Paragraph 2: life before television
Paragraph 3: television’s advantage over radio
Paragraph 4: television connects people globally
Paragraph 5: risks and responsibilities

Add a clear topic sentence to each paragraph.

Language and style issues

A. Word choice
There are several incorrect word choices:

“literal explosion of inventions”  
  “Explosion” is figurative here, not literal. Better: “an explosion of inventions” or “a rapid growth in inventions.”

“one lives easier”  
  Should be: “one’s life easier” or more naturally “people’s lives easier.”

“peak”  
  This should be “peek” if you mean “a quick look,” though even better would be “view” or “glimpse.”

“absorbtion”  
  Correct spelling: “absorption.”

“boarders”  
  You mean “borders.” (“Boarders” are people who rent rooms.)

B. Grammar
Watch for these patterns:

Apostrophes
“1940's” → “1940s”
“it’s happening” is correct only for “it is happening.” Be careful not to confuse its and it’s.

Articles
You often use “the television” where English usually prefers simply “television.”  
For example:
“The advent of the television” → “The advent of television”

Agreement and pronouns
“Some were created to make one lives easier” → “Some were created to make people’s lives easier”
“Besides the obvious higher level of enjoyment television watching may bring its viewer”  
  Better: “Besides the obvious enjoyment television brings to viewers…”

Sentence fragments
This is not a complete sentence:
> “A system that seemed to work…”

You need to connect it to a main clause:
> “This system seemed to work because many households owned or had access to a radio or newspaper…”

C. Punctuation and mechanics
Avoid using & in formal academic writing. Use “and.”

Also, some sentences are too long or loosely connected by commas. Divide them into shorter, clearer sentences.

For example:

Could become:
> “Like radio, television brought news and information into people’s homes. However, it added images, which greatly improved the audience’s understanding of what was being broadcast.”

---

Style advice for a German-speaking writer

German academic writing often allows longer sentences and more abstract phrasing. In English argumentative essays, clarity is usually stronger than complexity. So:
prefer shorter sentences,
use direct topic sentences,
avoid over-formal phrases if they sound unnatural,
support broad claims with specific examples.

---

Write a sharper thesis
Add 2–3 specific examples
Make each paragraph focus on one main idea
Correct grammar/spelling issues
Simplify long sentences for clarity

Feedback Report

Your essay presents a clear position: the personal computer is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed how people and businesses handle information. The topic is appropriate for an argumentative essay, and your main idea is understandable throughout. You also show good awareness of historical development, from large institutional computers to personal and portable computers. This gives your essay a logical direction.

Strengths

Clear focus  
   You stay on one main argument: computers transformed society by changing access to and processing of information.

Logical overall structure  
   The essay moves from general importance, to historical background, to the invention of the PC, and finally to its broader social effects.

Relevant examples  
   You mention practical uses such as writing papers, doing taxes, managing databases, and communicating through modems. These examples help make your point concrete.

Main areas to improve

Stronger thesis and clearer argumentation
At the moment, the essay is more explanatory than argumentative. You explain what computers do, but you do not fully develop a debate or defend your claim against alternatives.

For example, your introduction could state a sharper thesis:
*Among the inventions of the 20th century, the personal computer has had the greatest impact because it transformed work, communication, and access to information.*

Then each body paragraph should support one reason:
computers changed business,
computers changed private life,
computers increased access to information.

This will make the essay feel more like an argument and less like a description.

Paragraph unity and topic sentences
Some paragraphs are long and include many different ideas. Try to give each paragraph one central purpose.

For example, the long middle paragraph includes:
the IBM PC,
home use,
speed and software development,
modems,
portable computers,
phones and fax machines.

This is too much for one paragraph. Consider dividing it into two or three paragraphs.

Possible structure:
Paragraph 1: the arrival of the personal computer
Paragraph 2: how improvements in software and speed increased usefulness
Paragraph 3: communication and portability

Grammar and sentence structure
There are several grammar problems, especially with:
subject-verb agreement
article use
word order
punctuation
awkward phrasing

Examples:

“There has been many inventions”  
  → *There have been many inventions*

“business gathers, creates, responds to and, understands”  
  Remove the comma after *and*:
  → *business gathers, creates, responds to, and understands*

“It was mainly used though, by corporations and government”  
  Better word order:
  → *It was mainly used, however, by corporations and the government*

“with the use of Modem”  
  → *with the use of a modem*

“recieve”  
  → *receive*

“that before were not available”  
  → *that were previously not available*

As a German speaker, you may naturally write some long sentences with many linked clauses. In English academic writing, shorter and more direct sentences are often stronger.

Style and formality
Some phrases are repetitive or too conversational:
*just about anything*
*anything from almost anywhere*
*a very powerful tool indeed*

Try to use more precise academic language:
*a wide range of tasks*
*from many locations*
*an extremely influential technology*

Also, avoid direct address with “you” in formal essays unless necessary. Instead of:
*Today you can sit in your home...*
write:
*Today, users can create documents from home...*

Historical accuracy and wording
Be careful with factual claims. For instance:
*“IBM Corporation started a revolution… the invention of the personal computer”*  
IBM did not invent the personal computer in an absolute sense, though it played a major role in popularizing it. A more accurate wording would be:
*In 1980, IBM helped accelerate the personal computer revolution.*

Precision strengthens credibility.

Conclusion
Your conclusion restates the main idea, but it could be more forceful. Instead of only predicting the future, summarize why the computer matters most.

For example:
*In conclusion, the personal computer has had a greater impact than most other 20th-century inventions because it changed how people work, communicate, and access knowledge. Its influence continues to grow as modern life becomes increasingly dependent on digital information.*

Suggested revision priorities

Rewrite the introduction with a more direct thesis.
Divide the long body paragraph into smaller paragraphs.
Remove repetition and make vocabulary more precise.
Correct grammar and punctuation errors.
Make the essay more argumentative by organizing it around clear reasons.

Example of a revised opening

*There have been many inventions in the 20th century that changed everyday life, but few have had as much influence as the personal computer. In the information age, the computer has transformed how individuals, businesses, and governments create, store, and exchange information. Because it revolutionized work, communication, and access to knowledge, it can be considered one of the most important inventions of the century.*

Overall, this is a solid draft with a clear central idea. Your next step is to make the essay more focused, more formal, and more argumentative.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear central claim: the 20th century should be called “The Century of Democracy.” This is a strong argumentative idea, and your personal engagement gives the essay energy and sincerity. The reader can feel that this topic matters to you. For revision, focus on making the argument more precise, more historically accurate, and more formally structured.

Strengths

Engaged voice: The essay sounds passionate and confident.
Relevant historical examples: Germany, the Soviet Union, and the end of the Cold War all connect to your main argument.
Coherent ending: The conclusion returns to your main idea and gives the essay a hopeful final message.

Main revision priorities

A. Make the introduction shorter and more focused
Your first paragraph is interesting, but it spends too much time on general comments about decades (“Roaring 20s,” “Fabulous Fifties,” etc). This delays the main argument.

Suggestion: Move more quickly to your thesis. For example:
Briefly mention that the 20th century brought many major changes.
Then state that the spread of democracy was the most significant.

This will make your essay feel more academic and less like a speech.

B. Strengthen the argument with clearer reasoning
At the moment, some paragraphs describe events, but they do not always explain how they prove your thesis.

For each example, ask:
What happened?
How does it show the spread of democracy?
Why does this support calling the 20th century “The Century of Democracy”?

For instance, the paragraph about Germany should not only mention the Berlin Wall, but explain why German reunification symbolizes democratic change in Europe.

C. Be careful with overgeneralization
Some claims are too broad or too idealistic for an argumentative essay.

Examples:
“the world today… is becoming a better place to live for people of all nations”
“The idea of a nuclear war is practically non-existent today”
“if we all have one bond—democracy…”

These statements sound optimistic, but they are difficult to prove and may weaken your credibility. Try using more cautious language:

“to a greater extent than before”

This is the most important issue in your essay.

“the invention of the automobile” is not accurate for the 1920s. Cars became widespread then, but they were invented earlier.
“East and West Germany… unite as one democratic superpower” is problematic. Germany reunified, but it did not become a “democratic superpower.”
“the former superpower of the Soviet Union is now making its transition into a democracy” needs more precision. The Soviet Union dissolved, and several new states emerged; Russia attempted democratic reforms, but the process was complex.
“rid their nation of its communist government” oversimplifies events in the Soviet Union.

Your ideas are good, but you need more accurate wording. Historical imprecision can make the reader doubt the argument.

Introduction + thesis
Democratic change in Europe (Germany, Berlin Wall)

Conclusion

Style and tone

Your style is vivid, but sometimes too conversational for an academic essay.

Examples:
“How could we ever forget…”
“No, I’m not engulfed…”
“Perhaps…”

These rhetorical questions and personal comments create personality, but too many make the essay sound informal. You do not need to remove your voice entirely; just reduce these features and make the tone more analytical.

Grammar and language notes

Your English is generally strong, but here are some corrections and improvements:

“The ‘Roaring 20’s’” → “the Roaring Twenties”
“the 30’s” → “the 1930s”
“20th Century” → usually “twentieth century” in running text
“Perhaps, it is” → no comma: “Perhaps it is”

“more peaceful relations” → “more peaceful international relations”
“more worthwhile areas” → “more urgent domestic needs” or “social and economic development”

As a German speaker, pay special attention to capitalization and idiomatic phrasing. English uses fewer capitals than German.

Final advice

Shorten and sharpen the introduction

You have the basis for a compelling essay. With more precision and a more academic tone, your argument will become much stronger.

Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay has a clear purpose and a strong emotional voice. You want to warn readers about the dangers of nuclear energy and encourage political action. This gives your writing urgency, which is effective. You also use some strong contrasts, such as nuclear energy being both “awesome” and “terrible.” That said, to make the essay more convincing at a university level, you need to improve accuracy, structure, grammar, and evidence.

Main strengths
Clear position: Your opinion is easy to understand from the beginning.
Engaged tone: The essay shows commitment and concern, which can persuade readers emotionally.
Ambitious topic: You address an important global issue with ethical and environmental dimensions.

Main areas to improve

A. Make your argument more precise
At the moment, the essay mixes atomic bombs and nuclear energy too quickly, as if they are the same thing. They are related, but they are not identical. A stronger argument would:
distinguish between military use (nuclear weapons) and civilian use (nuclear power plants),
explain why both are dangerous,
and show more clearly why one should lead to opposition to the other.

Also, some claims are too broad or inaccurate. For example:
“Oppenheimer unveiled to the world his invention” is historically oversimplified.
“blowing holes in our sky” and linking nuclear energy to the ozone hole is not scientifically accurate.
“the world’s need for this dangerous energy augments” is vague and sounds exaggerated.

To persuade an academic reader, use specific, factual claims.

B. Strengthen the essay structure
Your essay has a strong opening, but the middle paragraphs repeat similar ideas. Try this structure:

Introduction
   - Introduce the topic.
   - Present your thesis clearly.
   - Example: *Although nuclear technology has provided energy and military power, its risks to humanity and the environment outweigh its benefits.*

Body Paragraph 1: Nuclear weapons
   - Explain destructive power.
   - Give historical evidence (Hiroshima, Nagasaki, arms race).

Body Paragraph 2: Nuclear energy
   - Discuss benefits briefly.
   - Then explain risks (accidents, radioactive waste, long-term contamination).

Body Paragraph 3: Counterargument
   - Acknowledge that some people support nuclear energy because it produces large amounts of electricity.
   - Refute this by arguing that safer alternatives exist.

Conclusion
   - Summarize your point.
   - End with a realistic call to action.

C. Use evidence, not only emotion
Your essay relies heavily on dramatic language, but an argumentative essay needs support:
examples of nuclear disasters (Chernobyl, Fukushima),
data on radioactive waste,
comparisons with renewable energy.

Without evidence, the reader may feel that the essay is passionate but not fully reliable.

Language corrections

Grammar and wording
Some sentences contain grammar or word choice problems. Here are useful corrections:

“The discovery of the Atomic energy, is undoubtedly the the most awesome…”
  - Better: “The discovery of atomic energy is undoubtedly one of the most significant and terrifying developments in human history.”
  - Notes:
    - no comma after *energy*
    - no double *the*
    - *atomic energy* should not be capitalized here

“The reason behind the fear that this energy causes is routed in…”
  - Better: “…is rooted in…”
  - This may be influenced by German; in English the correct phrase is rooted in.

“It is considered…”
  - Do not capitalize it in the middle of a sentence.

“the world’s need for this dangerous energy augments”
  - Better: “…increases” or “…continues to grow.”
  - *Augments* is not natural here.

“many other power source alternatives”
  - Better: “many alternative energy sources”

“various factors”
  - Better: “other factors” or “various causes”
  - *other various* is redundant.

“The lack of sight from our leaders”
  - Better: “The lack of foresight among our leaders”

“what is to become of our children.”
  - This should be a question: “What is to become of our children?”

“irreversable”
  - Correct spelling: irreversible

Style and punctuation
You often use short sentences for emphasis, which can work, but too many make the essay sound repetitive. Try combining some sentences to create a more academic style.

Also check:
unnecessary commas,
capitalization after colons,
spacing and paragraph formatting.

Advice especially relevant for a German-speaking writer
German writing often allows longer abstract phrasing, but English academic writing usually prefers:
clear topic sentences,
direct claims,
specific evidence,
less rhetorical exaggeration.

For example, instead of *“The current situation of the world’s environment is a sad one,”* write:
“The global environment is already under severe pressure from pollution, climate change, and industrial waste.”

This is more concrete and persuasive.

Final recommendation
Your essay has a strong moral argument, but to succeed academically it needs to become more balanced, fact-based, and logically structured. Revise by:
clarifying your thesis,
separating nuclear weapons from nuclear power,
adding evidence,
correcting grammar and word choice,
using a more formal academic tone.

You have a good foundation. With revision, this could become a much more convincing argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that television is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed how people receive information and understand the world. The essay is readable, and your examples about war coverage and the idea that television has “shrunk” the planet are strong starting points. You also show some balance by mentioning disadvantages. Below are suggestions to help you revise.

Strengths

Clear overall argument: from the beginning, the reader understands your main claim.
Relevant examples: the Vietnam War and international crises are useful examples of television’s influence.
Attempt at complexity: you do not present television as only positive; you also mention harmful effects.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction gives the general idea, but the thesis could be sharper. At the moment, you say television is “the most frequently used and influential invention of the field,” but “the field” is unclear. Also, your main reasons are not clearly listed.

A stronger thesis could be:
> Television is one of the most influential inventions of the 20th century because it made news immediate and visual, changed public reactions to war and global crises, and connected people to distant parts of the world.

This helps the reader see the structure of your essay.

B. Improve paragraph organization
Your body paragraphs contain good ideas, but each paragraph should have one clear main point.

Possible structure:
Introduction + thesis
Television makes information immediate and visual
Television influences public opinion, especially in war and humanitarian crises
Television connects the world but also has negative effects
Conclusion

Right now, some ideas are repeated or mixed together. For example, the point that television brings the world into the home appears in both the introduction and conclusion.

C. Develop your examples more fully
The Vietnam War example is interesting, but it needs more explanation. Instead of saying it “was one of the factors that led to much of the protesting,” explain why seeing graphic images changed public opinion. Similarly, the Somalia example is a bit brief. Add one sentence about how TV coverage increased international awareness or aid efforts.

D. Use more formal and natural phrasing
Some sentences sound awkward or overly complicated. This may come from direct translation patterns. Try to use simpler, more direct English.

Examples:
“has to do with the speed at which information is now communicated”  
  → concerns the speed at which information can now be communicated
“the most frequently used and influential invention of the field”  
  → the most influential invention in modern communication
“has made smaller the dimensions of the planet”  
  → has made the world seem smaller
“Not even truly dealing with the amount of television that is watched”  
  → Apart from the amount of television people watch

In academic English, clear phrasing is usually better than very long sentences.

Grammar and language corrections

Here are some specific issues to revise:

Comma/grammar:  
  “There are, however, several downsides to the television, and, like all inventions can be used in a useless way.”  
  → There are, however, several downsides to television, and like all inventions, it can be used in useless or harmful ways.

Article use:  
  In English, we usually say “television” rather than “the television” when speaking generally.

Verb tense consistency:  
  “television had changed people’s lives”  
  → television has changed people’s lives

Capitalization:  
  “the vietnam war”  
  → the Vietnam War

Word choice:  
  “graphically displayed” may suggest violent imagery. If you mean visually shown, use “shown visually” or “broadcast live with images.”

Sentence structure:  
  “We are able also to see for ourselves…”  
  → We are also able to see for ourselves…

Awkward phrase:  
  “Gone are the days of unknown and unheard of areas.”  
  → The days when many parts of the world were unknown to most people are gone.

Meaning/logic:  
  “the picture itself is not real” is not quite accurate. You may mean that viewers become detached from reality because they experience events through a screen.  
  → Television can desensitize viewers because repeated images of violence make real suffering seem distant or ordinary.

Style advice for a German-speaking writer

German academic writing often allows longer sentences and more abstract phrasing. In English argumentative essays, it is usually better to:
make the main point early in the sentence,
use shorter sentences,
avoid vague phrases such as “in a way”, “simple fact”, or “of the field”,
connect ideas clearly with words like first, for example, however, and therefore.

Final suggestion
For revision, focus first on:
writing a clearer thesis,
organizing each paragraph around one main idea,
simplifying awkward sentences,
correcting grammar and capitalization.

You already have a solid central idea. With clearer structure and more precise English, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Here is a feedback report to help you revise your essay.

Overall impression
Your essay has a clear topic and a strong main idea: the airplane changed people’s lives by making travel easier and increasing international contact. You also do something effective in argumentative writing: you connect the invention to larger social effects, not only to transportation itself. Your personal example about visiting Spain makes the essay more vivid and convincing.

To improve the essay, focus on three main areas:

sentence-level grammar and word choice
development of ideas with clearer examples

Thesis and organization

What works
Your introduction clearly states the invention you chose and suggests why it is important. The body paragraphs also follow a logical direction:
travel became easier
people gained broader cultural understanding
international relations and business improved

What to revise
Your thesis is still a little broad. Instead of saying the airplane changed lives in many ways, try to name the main reasons more precisely.

Possible revision of thesis
> The invention of the airplane has significantly changed people’s lives by making long-distance travel faster, increasing cultural exchange, and improving international business and political relations.

This thesis gives your essay a stronger structure, because each body paragraph can focus on one reason.

Paragraphing
Your second body paragraph includes several ideas at once: international relations, business, communication, and postal systems. Consider separating them or making the connections clearer. Each paragraph should have:
a topic sentence
explanation
an example
a concluding sentence

---

Grammar and word choice

Because you are a German-speaking writer, some of your sentences sound slightly literal or use vocabulary that is close but not fully correct in English. Below are the main issues.

A. Word choice
“The extend” → “The extent”
“astronomical” is understandable, but a more natural academic word would be “enormous” or “immense.”
“introduced… the concept of flying” sounds a little awkward, because humans already imagined flying before airplanes. Better:  
  > “made human flight a practical reality.”
“long distance traveling” → “long-distance travel”
“speeded up” → “sped up” is more natural

B. Articles and prepositions
These are common difficulties for German speakers.
“opened doors to traveling” → “opened the door to travel”
“the frequency that which foreign countries are able…” → this phrase is incorrect. Better:
  > “The frequency with which representatives of foreign countries can communicate and negotiate face to face…”

C. Natural English phrasing
Some sentences are grammatically possible but not idiomatic.
“The world has increased close contact between political systems”  
  Better:
  > “Air travel has increased contact among different political systems around the world.”
“Life with airplanes is more rapid paced.”  
  Better:
  > “As a result, modern life has become faster-paced.”

D. Redundancy
Avoid repeating similar words:
“also… also”
“extremely… extremely”
“The airplane has also basically speeded up the whole postal communication system also.”

A cleaner version:
> The airplane has also accelerated mail delivery and global communication.

---

Development of ideas

Strong point
Your personal example about Spain is one of the best parts of the essay because it shows real experience.

What to add
Some claims need more support. For example:
How exactly did airplanes improve international business?
In what way did they affect politics?
Can you mention tourism, migration, emergency aid, or global trade?

Adding one concrete example in each paragraph would make your argument stronger.

Example
Instead of:
> The invention of the airplane has opened many doors and one other door is international relations.

You could write:
> The airplane has also transformed international relations. Political leaders, diplomats, and businesspeople can now meet in person within hours instead of weeks, which makes negotiation, trade, and cooperation much easier.

---

Style and tone

Your tone is engaged and sincere, which is good. For a stronger academic style:
avoid very informal phrases like “a lot” or “basically”
use more precise verbs such as expanded, accelerated, enabled, transformed
keep the focus on analysis rather than personal opinion alone

For example:
“I have learned a lot” → “I have learned a great deal”
“very ignorant” → “unfamiliar” or “poorly informed” may sound more formal

---

Suggested revision priorities
If you revise this essay, I recommend this order:
Rewrite the thesis to make the three main arguments clear.
Improve paragraph structure so each paragraph has one central idea.
Correct grammar and awkward phrasing.
Add one or two specific supporting examples.
Proofread for small errors in word choice, punctuation, and repetition.

---

Final comment
This essay has a solid central argument and good potential. Your main ideas are interesting and relevant, and your personal experience adds authenticity. The next step is to make the essay more precise, more organized, and more natural in academic English. With revision, this could become a much stronger argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear purpose: you argue that, because medicine has not fully defeated AIDS, education is essential for prevention. That is a strong and relevant central idea. You also show awareness that fear and misinformation are part of the problem. For revision, focus on three areas: argument and organization, accuracy and tone, and language use.

Strengths
Clear topic: The essay stays focused on AIDS prevention.
Main claim is understandable: knowledge and education can help prevent the spread of AIDS.
Effort to persuade: you use examples and contrast fear with responsible behavior.

Main issues to revise

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your first paragraph introduces the topic, but the thesis is still broad. Try to state your position more directly.

Current idea:
medicine has advanced, but AIDS remains a threat
therefore education is important

Possible revised thesis:
Although medicine made important progress in the twentieth century, AIDS could not be controlled by treatment alone; therefore, public education became one of the most effective ways to prevent its spread.

This gives the essay a clear argument.

B. Improve organization
Right now, some ideas are repeated and the essay moves suddenly from one point to another. A clearer structure would help:

Introduction: background + thesis  
Body paragraph 1: limits of medical treatment  
Body paragraph 2: importance of education in schools and hospitals  
Body paragraph 3: examples of risky behavior and why knowledge matters  
Conclusion: restate argument and end with a strong final thought

Each body paragraph should begin with a topic sentence. Example:
Public education is necessary because many people do not understand how HIV is transmitted.

C. Be careful with factual accuracy and tone
Some phrases are too emotional, unclear, or medically inaccurate.

Examples:
“victims of AIDS” → better: “people living with HIV/AIDS”  
  (“Victims” can sound passive or stigmatizing.)
“it’s killing our population” → too dramatic; better: “it has affected millions of people worldwide.”
“dentists who ‘forget to put on their plastic gloves’” → this sounds casual and weakens credibility. If you mention medical transmission, use a more formal phrasing.
“random needles” → better: “shared or unsterilized needles.”

Also, avoid statements that may sound judgmental:
“selling their lives short” is not idiomatic here and sounds moralizing.  
  Better: “putting themselves at serious risk.”

D. Use more formal academic style
This essay should avoid informal abbreviations and symbols:
& → and
w/ → with
contractions such as it’s → it is

Your style should sound formal and objective.

Grammar and language corrections

A. Word choice and collocations
Some phrases sound unnatural in English:
“many ailing human beings” → “many sick people” or “many patients”
“seek other forms of comfort & rehabilitation” → “seek other forms of support and care”
“interact sexual” → “engage in sexual activity” or “have sexual contact”
“drugs effect” → “the effects of drugs”
“for the worst” → “for the worse”

B. Sentence structure and punctuation
You often connect ideas with semicolons where a period or comma would be better. For example:

Original:
“Due to the ineffectiveness of medicines; the population has needed…”

Correct:
“Due to the ineffectiveness of medicines, the population has had to…”

Also:
“If AIDS can not be prevented” → “If AIDS cannot be prevented”
“then they would realize” → better: “they would realize”
“The AIDS viruses was discovered” → “The AIDS virus was discovered”

C. Article and plural problems
Common for German speakers:
“the disease AIDS” is possible, but often “AIDS” alone is enough.
“many peoples lives” → “many people’s lives”
“support centers” may be okay, but “support groups” or “support services” sounds more natural.

Example of a revised sentence
Original:
“If AIDS can not be prevented by medication, it is possible that it can be prevented by knowledge.”

Improved:
“If AIDS cannot be fully prevented through medication alone, it can at least be reduced through education and public awareness.”

This version is clearer, more formal, and more precise.

Final advice for revision
When you revise, try to:
Write a clear thesis in the introduction.
Organize each paragraph around one main idea.
Use formal academic language.
Replace emotional or vague claims with specific, careful wording.
Check grammar, especially punctuation, articles, plurals, and word choice.

You have a strong core argument here. With better organization and more formal, accurate language, this essay could become much more convincing.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear personal voice and a strong sense of enthusiasm. It is easy to understand why contact lenses matter to you, and your examples from sports and daily life make the argument lively and relatable. This is a good foundation for an argumentative essay. For revision, I would focus on strengthening the argument, improving organization, and editing language and style.

Strengths

Engaging introduction:  
Your opening is energetic and immediately shows your position: contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century.

Relevant personal examples:  
The discussion of basketball, swimming, and morning routines helps readers see the practical advantages of contact lenses.

Clear stance:  
You consistently argue in favor of contact lenses and explain why they are superior to glasses for you.

Main revision priority: make it more argumentative

At the moment, the essay reads partly like a personal reflection or testimonial. That is useful, but an argumentative essay usually needs a broader claim supported by reasons that go beyond individual experience.

What to improve
Try to make your thesis more precise. For example:

not just: *contact lenses are important to me*
but: *contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because they improved mobility, safety, and quality of life for millions of people.*

Then organize your body paragraphs around clear reasons:
contact lenses improve participation in sports and physical activity  
they are more convenient in daily life than glasses  
modern developments such as soft and disposable lenses increased comfort and accessibility

You could also briefly acknowledge a counterargument, for example:
contact lenses can be expensive
some people find them uncomfortable
they require proper hygiene

Then explain why, despite these disadvantages, they remain highly valuable. This would make the essay sound more balanced and academically persuasive.

Organization

The essay generally follows a logical order, but some paragraphs could be more focused.

Suggestions
Introduction: Present the main claim more formally.
Body paragraph 1: Explain the limitations of glasses, especially for sports.
Body paragraph 2: Explain the development from hard lenses to soft lenses.
Body paragraph 3: Discuss disposable lenses and convenience.
Body paragraph 4: Optional: mention cosmetic uses, but connect this point more clearly to your argument.
Conclusion: Return to your thesis and explain why contact lenses matter not only to you but to society more generally.

The paragraph about colored lenses feels slightly disconnected. If you keep it, explain how this supports your main claim. Otherwise, you could remove it and expand a stronger point.

Style and tone

Your voice is vivid, but some expressions are too informal for academic writing.

Avoid:
contractions: *I’d, can’t, they’re*
symbols like *&*
conversational comments such as *which I must admit is a bit ridiculous!*
exclamations used too often

Use instead:
*I would*, *cannot*, *they are*
*and* instead of *&*
a more formal tone: *Although colored lenses may seem unnecessary to some people, they show that contact lenses also serve cosmetic purposes.*

Also, “glasses are a thing of the past” is too absolute, since many people still wear glasses. A more accurate conclusion would be better.

Grammar and vocabulary corrections

Here are some specific language issues to revise:

“a style called ‘hard lenses’ were” → *a style called “hard lenses” was*  
  (“style” is singular)

“lense” → *lens*  
  Singular = *lens*, plural = *lenses*

“caught rapidly” → *spread rapidly*  
  More natural collocation

“since the time I was 10” → *since I was ten years old*

“different colors for people who aren't happy with their own” → *different colors for people who are not satisfied with their natural eye color*

punctuation: You often use extra spaces, semicolons, dashes, and quotation marks in unusual ways. Simplify punctuation and use commas/full stops more consistently.

Example of a more academic thesis

Here is one possible revised thesis:

Contact lenses are one of the most significant inventions of the twentieth century because they allow greater freedom in physical activity, provide more convenience than glasses, and have improved the daily lives of millions of people.

This keeps your main idea but presents it in a more formal and argumentative way.

Final advice

When revising, ask yourself:
Am I arguing a general point, or only telling my personal story?
Does each paragraph support my thesis with one clear reason?
Is my tone formal enough for academic writing?
Have I checked grammar, spelling, and punctuation carefully?

You have a strong topic and a clear personal connection to it. With a more structured argument and a more formal style, this essay could become much more convincing.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0035.1.txt =====
Here is a feedback report to help you revise your essay.

Overall comments

Your essay has a clear central idea: feminism can be understood as an important “discovery” of the twentieth century because it changed how society thinks about women and equality. This is an interesting and thoughtful argument, and your introduction creates a strong comparison between technological discoveries and social change.

The essay is also well organized at the paragraph level: introduction, historical background, discussion of image/culture, and conclusion. This gives your reader a clear path through your ideas.

For revision, the main areas to improve are:

historical accuracy and precision
stronger argument development
grammar and word choice
more formal academic style

Strengths

Interesting thesis: Calling feminism a “discovery” is original and gives the essay a clear focus.
Logical structure: Each paragraph has a distinct purpose.
Awareness of complexity: You show that feminism involves both economics and cultural representation, not only legal rights.

Main revision priorities

Make the thesis more precise

At the moment, your essay suggests that feminism itself was “discovered” in the twentieth century. This is provocative, but it also risks sounding inaccurate, because feminism existed earlier. What seems closer to your meaning is that the twentieth century brought a new social recognition of women’s inequality and a stronger feminist movement.

You could revise your thesis to something like:

The twentieth century did not invent feminism, but it transformed it into a major social and political force that reshaped modern life.

This keeps your original idea but makes it more accurate.

Improve historical accuracy

Some claims are too general or factually uncertain:

“0th century” should be “20th century.”
“women winning the right to vote in the 1800s” is misleading in the U.S. context; women gained national suffrage in 1920.
“The first significant equal rights movement in the U.S. was in the 70s” is not correct. There were important movements much earlier, including the nineteenth-century suffrage movement.
The wage statistic (“60 cents for every dollar”) may be outdated or unsupported. In an academic essay, statistics should be accurate and ideally cited.

Because your essay depends on historical argument, accuracy is very important. If you do not want to include sources, at least use more careful phrasing, for example:

By the mid-twentieth century, traditional gender roles still dominated family life.
In the 1960s and 1970s, second-wave feminism brought new attention to legal, economic, and cultural inequality.

Develop your ideas with examples

Your argument would be stronger if each body paragraph gave one or two concrete examples.

For example:

In the paragraph on work and money, mention employment discrimination, unequal pay, or restricted career choices.
In the paragraph on image, give examples such as advertising, media stereotypes, or sexist language.

Right now, some points remain broad. Specific examples make your claims more convincing.

Language and style feedback

Grammar and usage

A few sentences show patterns that are common for German speakers, especially with article use and word choice.

Examples:

“modern day life” → “modern life”
“the field of technology is not the only one” → correct, but slightly heavy; “technology is not the only field” is smoother.
“it was still the most typical as well as the ideal” → better: “it was still considered both typical and ideal”
“housekeeping and tending the children” → “doing housework and caring for children”
“The first significant equal rights movement” → historically inaccurate, but also too absolute; “One important equal rights movement”
“things as varying as pornography or the language we use helps” → subject-verb agreement problem; “things as varied as pornography and the language we use help”
“breaking it down” is unclear because the pronoun “it” could refer to image or influence. Be more specific.

Punctuation

Do not put quotation marks around words unless necessary. In your essay, quotation marks around “discovery” and “discovered” are understandable, but overuse can make the tone seem uncertain. If this is your main concept, define it clearly instead of distancing it with quotation marks.

Also:
No comma after “discovered” in “It’s only recently that we’ve ‘discovered’, or begun…”
The sentence “Only when women have achieved equality with men, will there cease…” should not have a comma after “men.”

Correct version:
Only when women have achieved equality with men will there cease to be a need for feminism.

Suggested revision strategy

Rewrite the thesis so it is more precise.
Correct the historical points about suffrage and feminist movements.
Add concrete examples in each body paragraph.
Edit for style and grammar, especially article use, agreement, and sentence clarity.

Final thought

This essay has a strong core idea and good organization. Your main task now is to make the argument more accurate, more specific, and more academically polished. If you revise in those three areas, the essay will become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that care for the environment is one of the most important ideas of the 20th century. This is a strong topic for an argumentative essay because it is broad enough to develop with several examples. You also organize the body into different environmental concerns, which helps the reader follow your thinking.

What works well

Clear central idea
Your thesis appears in the first paragraph and stays consistent throughout the essay. The reader always knows what you are trying to prove.

Good range of examples
You mention several relevant issues:
nuclear weapons and nuclear waste
pollution of lakes and rivers
deforestation
fossil fuels
recycling
energy conservation
animal protection

This variety shows that you understand the topic from different angles.

Effective paragraphing
Each body paragraph focuses on one main issue. This gives the essay a logical structure.

---

Main areas to improve

Make the argument more precise
At the moment, the essay sometimes reads more like a list of environmental concerns than a developed argument about why this idea changed people’s lives most significantly.

Try to explain more clearly:
How did this idea change people’s daily lives?
Why is it more important than other 20th-century ideas?
What concrete effects did it have on laws, habits, education, or public opinion?

For example, instead of only saying people became aware of recycling, explain that this changed household behavior, government policy, and industry.

Strengthen topic sentences and development
Some paragraphs begin well but need fuller explanation. A good body paragraph should have:
a clear topic sentence
explanation
an example
a link back to your main argument

For instance, your recycling paragraph could explain not only what recycling is, but also why it represents a major social change in the 20th century.

Use a more formal academic style
Several expressions are too informal for an academic essay:
“&” → use and
“w/o” → use without
“b/c” → use because
“2” → use two

Also avoid conversational phrases such as:
“we are all aware”
“basically”
“fixed everything”

A more formal tone will make your argument stronger.

---

Language corrections

Grammar and word choice

Here are some important corrections:

“the importance of environment” → “the importance of the environment”
“countries that which have many weapons” → “countries that have many weapons”
“natural resources were never ending” → “natural resources were never-ending” or “would never run out”
“what we use, needs to be replaced” → “what we use needs to be replaced”
“people are more inclined to turn off their lights when they're not in their rooms”  
  Better: “people are more likely to turn off lights when they leave a room”
“We realize that conserving is not beneficial to our environment but it is also saves money.”  
  → “We realize that conservation is not only beneficial to the environment, but it also saves money.”
“more considerable of our animal life” → “more considerate toward animal life” or better “more concerned about animal life”
“hopeful by the 21st century” → “hopefully by the 21st century”

Sentence structure and punctuation

Some sentences are run-on sentences or comma splices. For example:

“For example deforestation is a very big issue, many groups have formed to plant more trees.”

This should be revised. Possible correction:
“For example, deforestation is a major issue, and many groups have formed to plant more trees.”

Also pay attention to transitions like for example, however, therefore, in addition. These will improve flow.

Pronoun consistency
You often use we, which can be acceptable, but in academic writing it is often better to use more neutral phrases such as:
people
society
many countries
governments and citizens

This avoids sounding too general.

---

Suggestions for revision

When revising, try this plan:

Introduction
State your thesis more strongly. For example:
*The idea of environmental responsibility changed people’s lives significantly in the 20th century because it influenced laws, everyday behavior, and attitudes toward natural resources and animals.*

Body paragraphs
For each paragraph:
State the environmental issue.
Explain how awareness of it changed society.
Give a specific example.
Connect it to your main claim.

Conclusion
Your conclusion is optimistic, which is good, but “fixed everything we have destroyed” is too exaggerated. End with a realistic final thought, such as:
*Although many environmental problems remain, the 20th century marked an important turning point in public awareness and responsibility.*

---

Final comment

This essay has a solid foundation: a clear topic, relevant examples, and recognizable structure. Your main revision goal is to turn the essay from a general discussion into a stronger argument with more explanation, more formal language, and more careful grammar. You already have good ideas; now focus on presenting them in a more precise academic way.

Strengths

   You do not stay too general. Your examples about school records, word processing, and airline booking help support your main claim.

Logical progression

Main areas to revise

Computers have transformed daily life by helping people store information, complete tasks more efficiently, and communicate or travel more easily.

This gives your essay a clearer direction.

Improve paragraph structure

Paragraph 1: introduction + thesis
Paragraph 2: how computers help you organize and process information

Conclusion: brief final statement about the broader importance of computers

This will make the essay easier to read and more persuasive.

Use a more formal academic style

& → use and
w/ → use with
w/out → use without
info → use information
on line → usually online

Also avoid conversational phrases such as:
“Well”
“no doubt”

These are acceptable in speech but sound less appropriate in formal writing.

Grammar and word choice corrections
There are several language errors, many of them small but important. Here are some key ones:

effected peoples lives → affected people’s lives  
  (“affected” is the verb; “effected” is usually incorrect here)

my life has change → my life has changed

before I had a computer → before I owned a computer

open to error → prone to errors or susceptible to error

had to this → had to do this

form the US → from the US

applied to the this program → applied to this program

would inevitably inundated → would inevitably be inundated

How did I get there?  
  Since you are already in Madrid, the more natural tense is How did I get here?

Sentence length and clarity
Many of your sentences are very long and include several ideas connected by commas and conjunctions. This can confuse the reader. Try to break long sentences into shorter ones.

For example, this sentence:
> “I'm able to store almost all the information I need for school & for other things in my computer whereas before I had a computer my desk was cluttered w/ paper & records of all sorts...”

Could become:
> “I am able to store almost all the information I need for school and other activities on my computer. Before I owned a computer, my desk was cluttered with papers and records of all sorts.”

Shorter sentences usually sound stronger and clearer.

Clarify your conclusion
Your final sentence is broad, but the conclusion would be more effective if it connected directly to your examples. For instance:
In conclusion, computers have become essential because they save time, reduce paperwork, and make everyday tasks much easier for individuals and institutions alike.

Suggested revision priorities
Replace informal abbreviations and symbols with full academic forms.
Correct grammar and spelling errors.
Divide long sentences and improve punctuation.
Add a clearer thesis in the introduction.
End with a more focused conclusion.

Example of a revised opening
> Computers have definitely affected people’s lives. In my own experience, owning a computer has changed the way I study, organize information, and complete everyday tasks. Computers have transformed daily life by allowing people to store information efficiently, perform tasks more accurately, and access services more conveniently.

This version is more formal, clearer, and more argumentative.

Overall, your essay has good ideas and useful examples. The main work now is to make the language more formal and accurate, and to organize the argument more clearly.

Feedback Report

You make a clear argument: the geostationary satellite is extremely important because it allows people around the world to witness events immediately. Your essay has energy, conviction, and a strong central example. The Tiananmen Square example is emotionally powerful and helps show why “nowness” matters. For revision, focus on three main areas: accuracy of language, organization, and formal academic style.

Strengths

Clear position: You strongly argue that the satellite is “the invention of the century.”
Relevant example: The China example supports your main idea well.
Strong voice: The essay sounds engaged and persuasive, which is good for argumentative writing.

Main revision priorities

A. Clarify the thesis
Your opening idea is interesting, but the thesis should be more precise and formal. Right now, the first paragraph contains several fragments and unclear phrases.

For example:
“Without question, the geo-centric satellite is the invention of the century.”

This is strong, but you should explain why in one sentence. For instance:
Possible revision: “The geostationary satellite can be considered one of the most important inventions of the century because it allows people worldwide to receive live images of events, shaping public awareness and political reaction.”

This makes the claim clearer and previews your reasons.

B. Improve paragraph structure
At the moment, the essay is mostly one long paragraph. Academic argument is easier to follow when each paragraph has one main purpose.

A possible structure:
Introduction: present the claim and main reasons.
Body paragraph 1: explain how satellites differ from radio, television, and videotape.
Body paragraph 2: discuss the Tiananmen Square example.
Conclusion: explain the broader importance of global visibility.

C. Use more formal academic style
Some phrases are too emotional or conversational for an academic essay:
“who wouldn't want to help his neighbor?”
“the satellite keeps a watchful eye on all of us”
“Plus when China is placed down the block”

These expressions are vivid, but they sound informal or rhetorical. In academic writing, it is better to argue directly and precisely.

For example:
Instead of: “who wouldn't want to help his neighbor?”
Try: “This increased sense of global proximity may encourage international solidarity.”

Language issues to correct

There are many spelling and word-choice errors. Here are important corrections:

geo-centric satellite → geostationary satellite or simply satellite
evention → inventions
preceed → precede
T.V. → television
invention possess → inventions possess
openned → opened
how the world functions → how the world functions
litterally → literally
Communism may not be the best word here; the Chinese government or state power is more accurate
Teniman Square → Tiananmen Square
Freedom US. the tank of injustice → this sentence is unclear and must be rewritten
he young hero → the young hero
a like → alike
mor → more
sensorship → censorship

Also watch subject-verb agreement:
“none of these invention possess” → “none of these inventions possesses”  
(or more naturally: “none of these inventions offer...”)

Sentence-level problems

Some sentences are fragments or difficult to understand:

“As it happens- the most profound byproduct of the satellite.”
  - This is a fragment.
  - Possible revision: “Its most profound contribution is the ability to show events as they happen.”

“Freedom US. the tank of injustice made its way into the lives of most of the world's people.”
  - This is not understandable in its current form.
  - You probably mean that the image of the student facing the tank was seen worldwide.
  - Possible revision: “Images of the student standing in front of a tank brought the struggle for freedom into homes across the world.”

“Without the satellite, the movement for democracy... would have been lost due to the stagnation of time.”
  - “stagnation of time” is not idiomatic English.
  - Possible revision: “Without satellite broadcasting, the movement for democracy might not have received immediate global attention.”

Argumentation: be careful with overstatement
You sometimes make claims that are too absolute:
“the invention of the century”
“the truth was told”
“governments and people alike will be less tempted to do wrong”

Strong arguments are more convincing when they are qualified:
Try words like: “arguably,” “significantly,” “may,” “can,” “helped to”

This does not weaken your essay; it makes it sound more credible.

Suggested revised thesis

Final advice
For your next draft:
Divide the essay into clear paragraphs.
Rewrite unclear sentences completely rather than only correcting small words.
Use formal vocabulary instead of rhetorical questions and dramatic phrases.
Proofread carefully for spelling and grammar.
Keep your strong main idea and your China example—they are the best parts of the essay.

You have an interesting argument with real persuasive potential. With clearer structure and more precise English, this could become a strong academic essay.

Overall comments

---

Argument and organization

What works

What to improve

How to revise
Make your thesis more precise in the introduction. For example:

Possible revised thesis:

This thesis is clearer because it tells the reader exactly what the essay will argue.

Also, make sure each paragraph clearly supports this thesis:
Paragraph 1: computers in business and administration
Paragraph 2: computers and student writing
Paragraph 3: computers as learning tools

Your conclusion should not introduce vague new ideas (“who knows where these machines will take us”). Instead, restate your main argument more confidently.

---

Some points need more explanation.

Example

This sounds more accurate and academic.

In the paragraph about teaching, your ideas are interesting, but the example “cosm graph in Mathematics” is unclear. Try to use a more specific and familiar example, such as graphs, simulations, or language-learning programs.

---

Language, grammar, and word choice

There are several grammar and vocabulary problems, many of which are common for learners of English.

Common issues in your essay

a) Subject-verb agreement
“Technology is improving every day” — correct
“they is clearly imp” — incorrect

Correct version:
*they are clearly important*

b) Article use
English requires articles more often than German.
“with respect to the people” → awkward
“the use of a card catalog is no longer used” → repetitive

Better:
*For individuals, computers have also had a tremendous influence.*  
*Card catalogs are no longer commonly used in libraries.*

c) Word form and collocation
Some expressions are understandable but not natural in English:
“god send” → godsend
“filed on disks” → stored on disks
“paper writing” → writing papers
“teaching instruments” → teaching tools

d) Informal abbreviations
Avoid informal forms in academic writing:
“w/o” → without
“imp” → important

---

Sentence clarity and style

Some sentences are too long or awkwardly constructed. Shorter, clearer sentences will improve your essay.

Example
Original:

Possible revision:

Another example:
*They have enabled to students to learn in more ways than were previosly used*

*They have enabled students to learn in ways that were not previously available.*

There are several spelling mistakes:
definantly → definitely
accesible → accessible
previosly → previously
extend → extent
incredible useful → incredibly useful

---

Suggested revision priorities

Revise each body paragraph so it clearly supports one main point.
Use more formal academic language.
Correct grammar and spelling errors.
Strengthen the conclusion by summarizing your argument.
